L352: ## 5. Pharmacologic Activity, Pharmacokinetics, and Clinical Pharmacology
L353: ## 5.1. Nonclinical Assessment of Potential Effectiveness
L354: ## 5.1.1. Primary Pharmacology
L355: Concizumab is a humanized recombinant monoclonal antibody (mAb) that inhibits the function of TFPI\alpha and TFPI\beta, that represent the two major TFPI-1 isoforms (splice variants) involved in the down regulation of the coagulation cascade in humans. Concizumab binds specifically to the Kunitz-domain-2 (K2) of the TFPI and prevents TFPI from binding to and blocking the active site of the coagulation factor Xa (Fxa). When the TFPI inhibitory activity is reduced, the Fxa produced by the coagulation factor VIIa (FVIIa)/tissue factor (TF) complex results in sufficient generation of thrombin to achieve hemostasis. The mechanism of action of concizumab is independent of the presence or absence of FVIII or FIX, and would exert pharmacodynamic activity in both hemophilia A and B patients, regardless of their inhibitor status. Concizumab does not bind to the human TFPI-2 protein.
L356: **Figure 1. Mechanism of Action (Concizumab)**
L357: Source: Applicant's Figure Nonclinical Overview
Abbreviations: a, activated; TF, tissue factor; TFPI, tissue factor pathway inhibitor
L358: The pharmacological activity of concizumab was confirmed by thromboelastography (TEG), that measures the shear elasticity and the dynamics of clot formation and the strength and stability of clot formation, using blood derived from humans and cynomolgus monkeys. Concizumab reduced clotting times and improved clot development in a concentration-dependent manner in both human and cynomolgus monkey whole blood. Thromboelastography (TEG) conducted using human whole blood under antibody-induced hemophilia A-like conditions resulted in half maximal effective concentration (EC50) values of 0.43nM (clot time) and 2.2nM (clot development), and under antibody-induced hemophilia B-like conditions resulted in EC50 values of 0.44nM (clot time) and 2.6nM (clot development). Comparable results were observed in whole blood derived from normal cynomolgus monkeys under antibody-induced hemophilia A-
L359: like conditions where the EC50 values were 0.38nM (clot time) and 0.54nM (clot development). The in vitro, ex vivo and in vivo pharmacology studies are discussed in further detail under Section 13.1.1.1.
L360: Concizumab neutralized TFPI-mediated inhibition of Fxa and the FVIIa/TF/Fxa complex (in vitro) with EC50 values of 0.8nM and 1.1nM, respectively. Neutralization of plasma TFPI was further quantified by measuring TFPI activity in human plasma and assessing FX activation in vitro (EC50 value, 6nM). These effects occurred at clinically relevant exposures based on the steady state concizumab concentrations (maximum plasma concentration (Cmax), mean) observed in hemophilia patients during the Explorer7 study (1167 ng/mL = 7.83nM). Concizumab was effective at neutralizing TFPI present on the surface of endothelial cells (the predominate form of TFPI on the cell surface of endothelial cells is TFPI\beta). Concizumab associated with the cell surface (KD values, 0.1nM to 0.4nM) of endothelial cells (HUVEC) and endothelial-like cells (ECV304), and promoted activation of FX (EC50 values were below 0.5nM for both cell lines).
L361: Based on the pharmacology data and the lack of cross reactivity to rodent species, the rabbit was selected as the nonclinical efficacy species (hemophilia animal models). The cynomolgus monkey was selected as the non-rodent nonclinical safety species for repeat-dose toxicology and both species were used in pharmacokinetic/pharmacodynamic (PD) assessments.
L362: ## 5.1.2. Animal Model Data Showing Proof of Concept
L363: In vivo proof-of-concept studies were conducted in a cuticle bleeding model in New Zealand White (NZW) rabbits where hemophilia A-like conditions were simulated by administration of a neutralizing FVIII antibody. When administered intravenously (IV) prior to induction of bleeding, concizumab reduced blood loss in a dose-dependent manner in rabbits with a half maximal effective dose (ED50) of 0.23 mg/kg (0.074 mg/kg, human equivalent dose (HED)) over the 1-hour observation period. The antihemorrhagic effect was maintained for up to 7 days after a single IV dose of 20 mg/kg (6.5 mg/kg, HED) followed by treatment (on Day 4, 7 or 10) with a neutralizing FVIII antibody to induce pseudo hemophilia. The absence of a notable PD effect on Day 10 corresponded to a significant decline in plasma concizumab exposure at the same timepoint. Procoagulant effects were not observed when concizumab was administered (IV) after an extended bleed induction period (15-30 min), indicating that concizumab needs to be administered prior to or relatively soon after (\le5 minutes) injury to be efficacious in this rabbit model of HA. Concizumab was also effective after subcutaneous (SC) administration (24 hours prior to induction of bleeding using the clinical ROA) in the rabbit cuticle bleeding model at a dose of 10 mg/kg (3.2 mg/kg, HED).

L373: The effects of concizumab on local clot formation and general coagulation activation were assessed in a venous stasis model conducted in non-hemophilic NZW rabbits. There were no notable differences in the number of clots or clot size in ligated facial vein segments, and no significant differences in platelet counts, prothrombin time (PT), thrombin-anti-thrombin (TAT), and fibrinogen levels found in the absence or presence of concizumab (2 mg/kg, IV). Lung thrombi were noted microscopically in 50% of the rabbits administered concizumab and in the heart of a single control rabbit (25%). The relationship of distal clot formation in the lung and heart to the clot formation in the ligated facial vein segments could not be definitively determined, and while the Applicant argues that the formation of lung thrombi may be related to the experimental procedures, a potential relationship to the administration of concizumab cannot be excluded.
L374: In a second study, the effect of a low molecular weight heparin (LMWH) in combination with concizumab was evaluated in a modified venous injury model in non-hemophilic NZW rabbits to assess the ability of LMWH to interfere with concizumab-induced clot formation. Intravenous injection (2 mg/kg) of concizumab was followed by a standardized crush injury and eventual ligation of the facial veins. Local clot formation following crush injury was significantly increased by the presence of concizumab; however, when followed 5 minutes later by the administration of LMWH prior to the initiation of the crush injury, local clot formation was prevented. Activated Partial Thromboplastin Time (APTT) declined minimally in concizumab-only dosed rabbits and APTT clotting time could not be established in the presence of LMWH. No other notable changes in coagulation parameters (platelet counts, PT, and fibrinogen) were observed with concizumab dosing, and these results suggest that LMWH can prevent the
L375: procoagulant effects of concizumab in normal (non-hemophilic) NZW rabbits. These studies are discussed in detail under Section 13.1.1.3.

L383: ## 5.2. Clinical Pharmacology / Pharmacokinetics
L384: **Table 8. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetics**
L385: Characteristic	Drug Information
Established pharmacologic class (EPC)	Concizumab is a tissue factor pathway inhibitor (TFPI) antagonist.
Mechanism of action	Concizumab is a humanized recombinant monoclonal antibody (IgG4) antagonist of human tissue factor pathway inhibitor 1 (TFPI-1), including TFPI\alpha and TFPI\beta, that represent the two major TFPI-1 isoforms found in humans. Concizumab specifically targets the Kunitz 2 (K2) domain of TFPI and prevents TFPI from interacting with the active site of the Stuart-Prower factor [i.e., activated blood coagulation factor X (Fxa)]. In the presence of reduced TFPI inhibitory activity, the Fxa produced by the activated factor VII (FVIIa)/tissue factor (TF) complex (extrinsic tenase) results in generation of thrombin. As the mechanism of action of concizumab does not require the presence of FVIII or FIX, concizumab has the potential to elicit hemostatic activity in patients with either hemophilia A (congenital FVIII deficiency) or hemophilia B (congenital FIX deficiency), regardless of their inhibitor (anti-clotting factor antibody) status.
Active moieties	The active moiety is the (b)(4) humanized Immunoglobulin G (IgG) 4 monoclonal antibody (mAb).
QT prolongation	A thorough QT study was not conducted in humans. QT prolongation was not observed during ECG assessments in cynomolgus monkeys administered repeat doses of concizumab up to 200 mg/kg/once weekly (IV), 70000x MRHD (AUC) and 10000X MRHD (C_{max}), or 50 mg/kg/day (SC), 14000x MRHD (AUC) and 12600X MRHD (C_{max}), for up to 13 weeks. In addition, QT prolongation was not observed during ECG assessments in cynomolgus monkeys administered repeat doses of concizumab up to 9 mg/kg/day (SC), 4300x MRHD (AUC) and 4400X MRHD (C_{max}), for up to 52 weeks.
Bioanalysis	Total plasma concizumab (bound and unbound) concentration in the Phase 3 study was quantified using a validated ELISA.
Healthy subjects versus patients	Based on the popPK analysis, steady-state average concizumab plasma concentrations in healthy subjects are 55% higher (90% CI: 0%, 183%) than that in hemophilia A patients with inhibitors following subcutaneous (SC) administration of 0.20 mg/kg concizumab.
Drug exposure at steady state following the therapeutic dosing regimen (or single dose, if more relevant for the drug)	In Study 4311, the steady-state geometric mean (CV%) C_{max,ss} and C_{trough,ss} of concizumab were 1167 (130%) ng/mL and 665 (220%) ng/mL at Week 24.
Range of effective dose(s) or exposure	Based on PK/PD (free TFPI, peak thrombin) and exposure-response (ABR) relationships, concizumab steady-state trough concentrations above 200 ng/mL are effective.
Maximally tolerated dose or exposure	Based on concizumab concentrations observed in the 3 patients with thrombotic events from the Phase 3 studies, maintenance doses are adjusted to keep concizumab trough concentrations less than 4000 ng/mL.
Dose proportionality	Concizumab concentrations increased with increasing dose in a greater-than-dose-proportional manner following SC administration. Relative to the 0.25 mg/kg dose, geometric mean AUC_T values were 3-fold higher in the 0.50 mg/kg dose and nearly 11-fold higher in the 0.80 mg/kg dose.
L386: Characteristic	Drug Information
Accumulation	Due to a loading dose on Day 1, the exact magnitude of drug accumulation upon once daily dosing was not determined. In the absence of a loading dose, simulated drug exposure at steady-state may be 10-fold higher than on Day 1 following a daily dose of 0.25 mg/kg concizumab
Time to achieve steady-state	Following a single concizumab loading dose of 1 mg/kg, the geometric mean concizumab plasma concentrations reached steady-state levels by the end of Day 1. Without a loading dose, it may take more than a week to reach to steady state exposures.
Bridge between to-be-marketed and clinical study formulations	N/A as the to-me-marketed formulation and pen injector were used in the registration study.
Absorption
Bioavailability	Cannot be estimated with available data
Tmax	Following SC administration of concizumab at 0.25 mg/kg, the median Tmax ranges from 12 to 24 hours.
Food effect (fed/fasted)	N/A as concizumab is administered via SC injection.
Geometric least square mean and 90% CI	
Distribution
Volume of distribution	The volume of distribution of concizumab for a typical 70 kg patient was 2.96 L.
Plasma protein binding	N/A
Drug as substrate of transporters	N/A as concizumab is a monoclonal antibody
Elimination
Mass balance results	N/A
Clearance	Both linear and non-linear pathways contribute to the elimination of concizumab. This non-linear pharmacokinetic behavior is caused by target-mediated drug disposition (TMDD) which occurs when concizumab binds to endothelial cell-anchored TFPI with subsequent elimination of the drug-target complex. Non-linear PK via TMDD plays an important role in concizumab elimination at the clinically relevant concentrations.
Half-life	Concizumab terminal half-life increased with increasing dosage and ranged from 1.6 days to 8.1 days in healthy and hemophilia subjects receiving a single SC dosing of 0.25 to 3 mg/kg. In the target exposure range of 200 to 4000 ng/mL, the effective half-life is about 1-2 days.
Metabolic pathway(s)	Concizumab is expected to be metabolized into small peptides by catabolic pathways.
Primary excretion pathways (% dose)	N/A
L387: Characteristic	Drug Information
Intrinsic Factors and Specific Populations
Body weight	The apparent clearance and volume of distribution of concizumab increased with increasing body weight (27 kg to 130.7 kg). Nonetheless, after 4 weeks of treatment, patients with low or high exposure should adjust dose to 0.25 or 0.15 mg/kg, respectively. No further adjustment to dose based on body weight is warranted beyond the recommended adjustment by Week 8.
Age	No clinically significant differences in the PK of concizumab were observed based on age (12 year to 67 years).
Renal impairment	No dedicated studies have been conducted to evaluate the impact of renal impairment. As concizumab is a monoclonal antibody, there is no expectation for concizumab exposures to be different in patients with renal impairment.
Hepatic impairment	No dedicated studies have been conducted to evaluate the impact of hepatic impairment. As concizumab is a monoclonal antibody, there is no expectation for concizumab exposures to be different in patients with hepatic impairment.
Drug Interaction Liability (Drug as Perpetrator)
Inhibition/induction of metabolism	N/A as concizumab is a monoclonal antibody
Inhibition/induction of transporter systems	N/A as concizumab is a monoclonal antibody
Immunogenicity
Bioanalysis	Anti-concizumab antibody assay and in vitro neutralizing antibody assay have been reviewed by Dr. Scott Walsh and were considered as not fully validated (see comments in the CR letter for details)
Incidence	In concizumab clinical program, 47 out of 185 of treated patients (25%) developed anti-concizumab antibodies. Twelve (12) patients (6.5%) tested positive for anti-concizumab antibodies with in vitro-neutralizing effects.
Clinical impact	Binding anti-concizumab antibody titers were generally low. In 1 of 47 patients who developed ADAs with a high titer level, plasma concizumab concentrations cannot be measured, likely due to ADA interference in the PK. In addition, free TFPI levels returned to the baseline, indicating a loss of treatment effect. For the rest of 46 ADA positive patients, there was no identified clinically significant effect of the antibodies on PK, PD, safety, or effectiveness of concizumab.
L388: Source: Reviewers created table
L389: Abbreviations: ABR, annualized bleeding rate; ADA, anti-drug antibody; AUC, area under the time-concentration curve; CI, confidence interval; Cmax, maximum plasma concentration; CR, complete response; Ctrough, trough concentration; ECG, electrocardiogram; CV, coefficient of variation; ELISA, enzyme-linked immunoassay; EPC, established pharmacologic class; FVIIa, activated factor VII; Fxa, coagulation factor Xa; IgG4, immunoglobulin G4; IV, intravenous; mAb, monoclonal antibody; MRHD, maximum recommended human dose; N/A, not applicable; PD, pharmacodynamic; PK, pharmacokinetic; popPK, population pharmacokinetics; SC, subcutaneous; ss, steady state; TF, tissue factor; TFPI, tissue factor pathway inhibitor; Tmax, time to maximum concentration; TMDD, target mediated drug disposition
L390: ## 6. Efficacy (Evaluation of Benefit)
L391: ## 6.1. Assessment of Dose and Potential Effectiveness
L392: The proposed dosing regimen of concizumab is a loading dose of 1 mg/kg via SC administration on Day 1 followed by once daily dose of 0.20 mg/kg starting from Day 2 until individuation of maintenance dose is determined:
L393: - Four weeks after initiation of treatment: For dose optimization measure concizumab plasma concentration once by concizumab enzyme-linked immunoassay (ELISA) prior to administration of next scheduled dose.
- After concizumab plasma concentration result is available, but recommended no later than 8 weeks after initiation of treatment: Individualize maintenance dose of concizumab once based on the following concizumab plasma concentrations:&lt;200 ng/mL: adjust to a once-daily dose of 0.25 mg/kg
- 200 to 4000 ng/mL: continue once-daily dose of 0.20 mg/kg
- &gt;4000 ng/mL: adjust to a once-daily dose of 0.15 mg/kg
L394: The proposed PK-guided dosing with one time PK collection and dose adjustment between weeks 4 to 8 was implemented in Study 4311 (after restart) and is considered acceptable based on favorable efficacy and safety results, aided by pharmacokinetic/pharmacodynamic (PK/PD) and exposure-response relationships in patients with hemophilia. Large fluctuations in concizumab plasma concentrations were noted in certain patients in Study 4311 (after restart) which may be due to an interplay of concizumab's target mediated drug disposition (TMDD) and patient noncompliance. It is critical that patients are compliant with administering concizumab daily (See Section 7.2 for more details).
L395: ## Selection of Concizumab Target Exposure of 200 ng/mL to 4000 ng/mL
L396: The Applicant proposes that concizumab dose be adjusted once to achieve target trough concentration between 200 and 4000 ng/mL. The selection of lower exposure limit of 200 ng/mL is based on exposure-response analysis using data from main part of Studies 4255 and 4310. As shown in Table 9, at concentrations above 200 ng/mL, there was a trend towards a lower bleeding rate.
L397: The upper exposure limit of 4000 ng/mL has been set as a measure to avoid patients persistently reaching very high concentrations of concizumab. Before the pause of Study 4311, 2 of the 3 patients with thrombotic events in phase 3 studies showed exposure levels above 5000 ng/mL (Figure 2). Despite the lack of data showing that high exposure per se is causative of thrombotic events, the observation suggests that the concizumab exposure in combination with other risk factors could contribute to thrombosis. Therefore, to avoid patients reaching very high concizumab exposure levels, the Applicant proposed a cut-off of 4000 ng/mL at Week 4. The selected target exposure of 200 to 4000 ng/mL was agreed by the Agency and implemented in Study 4311 after the trial was restarted.
L398: **Table 9. Predicted Annualized Bleeding Rates for Different Concizumab Exposure Intervals Based on Results From Main Parts of Studies 4255 and 4310**
L399: Concizumab exposure (ng/mL)	Number of subjects	Subjects with bleeds	Number of bleeds	Total exposure years	ABR
0–100	53	30	71	6.64	10.7
100–200	47	29	88	8.96	9.82
200–500	25	11	19	5.05	3.77
500–1000	13	5	9	2.32	3.88
>1000	8	3	5	1.04	4.81

L402: **Figure 2. Observed Exposure of Concizumab After a Loading Dose of 1 mg/kg and a Daily Maintenance Dose of 0.25 mg/kg Concizumab for Patients With Thrombotic Events Versus Those Without, Studies 4311 and 4307**
L403: Source: Type A meeting package dated on May 14th, 2020, Figure 17.
L404: Abbreviation: TE, thrombotic event
L405: ## **Necessity of PK-Guided Dosing for Concizumab**
L406: Both linear and non-linear clearance pathways contribute to the elimination of concizumab. At concentrations of approximately 10000 ng/mL or above, the linear clearance pathway is dominant, and the estimated half-life is approximately 22 days for a 75 kg patient based on population PK modeling analysis. Over concizumab's target exposure range of 200 to 4000 ng/mL, the non-linear clearance pathway via TMDD is dominant and the effective half-life of concizumab is much shorter, about 1 to 2 days. As a result, large between-subject variations in concizumab exposure are expected due to variability in target pool (i.e., membrane bound TFPI in the endothelium) among individual patients.
L407: Population PK simulations suggest that a dose increase to 0.25 mg/kg or decrease to 0.15 mg/kg can reduce the population-level variability and ensure that majority of patients have concizumab trough concentrations within the target range of 200 to 4000 ng/mL (Figure 3). In Study 4311,
L408: out of the 97 patients with maintenance dosing based on PK concentrations at visit 4, 72 patients (74.2%) remained on the 0.20 mg/kg dose level, 24 patients (24.7%) increased to 0.25 mg/kg and 1 patient (1.0%) decreased to 0.15 mg/kg. Based on data from Study 4311 (after restart), concizumab when administered according to the proposed dosing regimen was effective in reducing the ABR compared to no prophylaxis (PPX) in patients with HAwI and HBwI across all bleed-related efficacy endpoints and assessments (See Table 13 under Section 6.2.1.5). No thrombotic events occurred with PK-guided dosing.
L409: **Figure 3. Simulated Concizumab Exposure Following a Loading Dose of 1 mg/kg and Initial Daily Dose of 0.2 mg/kg Without (A) and With (B) One-time Dose Adjustment at Week 6 According to the Proposed PK-Guided Dosing Criteria**
L410: Source: Population PK and exposure-response meta-analysis of Phase 1, 2 and 3 data Figure 1-2. Simulated based on the final model (10,000 subjects simulated). Shaded area represents the 90% prediction interval, and dark line represents the median exposure levels. Dotted lines indicate the exposure range used for maintenance dose setting in Study 4311. Maintenance dose setting was implemented as a dose increase or decrease at Week 6, based on Ctrough values occurring at Week 4.
L411: Abbreviations: Ctrough, trough concentration; PK, pharmacokinetics
L412: ## **Rationale for Loading Dose**
L413: Following a single loading dose of 1 mg/kg, the geometric mean concizumab plasma concentrations reached steady-state levels by end of Day 1 and stayed within the target exposure range with daily maintenance doses. This observation was further supported by population PK predicted concizumab plasma concentration-time profile with a loading dose of 1 mg/kg on Day 1 (Figure 3 and Figure 4).
L414: **Figure 4. Concizumab PK Following 0.25 mg/kg Daily Dose With or Without a Loading Dose of 1 mg/kg (Simulation)**
L415: Source: Figure 20 of EOP2 meeting package dated 01 February 2019
Abbreviation: PK, pharmacokinetics
L416: ## **Rationale for Once-Daily Dosing**
L417: Study 4159 assessed the PK and PD of concizumab following SC administration of 0.25 mg/kg, 0.5 mg/kg and 0.8 mg/kg doses given every 3 days. This is equivalent to 0.83, 0.17 and 0.27 mg/kg/day. Because clearance is much faster in the target range (as stated earlier), a once-in-3-day dosing is expected to result in larger peak-to-trough fluctuations than desired. Combined with high inter-subject variability (as noted in the study), this regimen may put some patients at risk of going below or above the target range. Therefore, in order to reduce peak-to-trough fluctuations and maintain exposures within the target range in majority of the patients, a once-daily dosing was implemented.
L418: ## **Exposure-Response and PK/PD Analysis Supporting the Proposed Dosing Regimen**
L419: An exposure-response analysis of bleeding episodes shows that the concizumab dosing regimen maintains plasma concentrations at or near the plateau of effect (Figure 5). The maximum drug effect (Emax) was predicted to be a 93% reduction in ABR with an EC50 around 45.2 ng/mL. In addition, the correlation between pre-dose free TFPI and concizumab plasma concentration confirms that the most prominent effect is observed at concizumab plasma levels &gt;200 ng/mL with free TFPI approaching the lower limit of quantification (9.6 ng/mL, Figure 6). Similarly, PK/PD analysis of thrombin peak shows that the concizumab dosing regimen maintains plasma concentrations at the plateau of effect.

L422: The line is the mean model-based prediction. The horizontal light blue line at the bottom represents the median (diamond) and the 5-95% range of the individual average exposure predicted across the study duration (only including data after restart of Trial 4311), using C_{\text{avg, study}} estimated from the BOV PK model.

L428: ## 6.2. Clinical Studies Intended to Demonstrate Efficacy
L429: ## 6.2.1. Study 4311
L430: ## 6.2.1.1. Study Design, Study 4311
L431: ## Study Overview
L432: Study 4311 was a Phase 3 prospective, multicenter, open-label, randomized-controlled study in patients with HAwI and HBwI. The primary objective of the study was to compare concizumab prophylaxis to no prophylaxis on top of standard of care (on demand therapy) in preventing bleeding episodes in patients with HAwI and HBwI.
L433: The schema for Study 4311 is shown in Figure 7 below and included 4 study arms. Patients were randomized to concizumab PPX (arm 2), no PPX (arm 1), or assigned to non-randomized concizumab PPX treatment (arms 3 or 4), based on their treatment regimen for hemophilia before entering the study. Patients included in the no PPX arm (arm 1) of the study continued the same on-demand treatment regimens with bypassing agents that they received before entering the clinical study, reflecting local standards of care. Patients randomized to the concizumab arm were permitted to use on demand treatment(s) (i.e., bypassing agents and factor) as needed for breakthrough bleeding. Arm 3 included patients who were previously enrolled in the concizumab Phase 2 study (NN7415-4310 (explorer4)). Arm 4 included patients previously on prophylaxis with bypassing agents as well as any patients receiving on-demand therapy who were screened at a timepoint where the required number of patients in arms 1 and 2 had been randomized.
L434: The study consisted of a 3-week screening period, a main part (study period between Weeks 24-32), an extension part (study period between Weeks 128-136), and a 7-week follow-up period. The main part of the study was considered completed for a patient when at least 24 weeks of participation (screening period not included) had been completed for patients on no PPX (arm 1), or 32 weeks of participation (screening period not included) for patients on concizumab PPX (arms 2, 3 and 4). The longer duration of the study's main part for arms 2, 3 and 4 was due to the dose adjustment period in these. After the main part of the study, all patients were offered opportunities to continue in the extension part of the study and receive treatment with concizumab for up to an additional 128 weeks (arms 2-4) or 136 weeks (arm 1). A primary analysis cut-off (PACO) was defined as when all patients on no PPX (arm 1) had completed visit 9/9a (or withdrawn), and all patients on concizumab PPX (arm 2) had completed visit 10a (or withdrawn). Furthermore, a 56-week cut-off was defined as when all patients on concizumab PPX (arms 2, 3 and 4) had completed visit 13a (or permanently discontinued treatment). At this 56-week cut-off, an additional evaluation was planned to assess bleed-related endpoints and safety.
L435: The randomization between treatment arms 1 and 2 was stratified according to hemophilia subtype (HAWI, HBWI) and bleeding frequency during the 24 weeks prior to screening (&lt;9 bleeding episodes, ≥9 bleeding episodes).
L436: ## Treatment Pause
L437: Study 4311 was initiated in October 2019. Concizumab treatment was paused in March 2020, and clinical studies were subsequently put on clinical hold due to the occurrence of 5 non-fatal thromboembolic events in 3 patients (see Section 7.7.1 for further details). Patients who were randomized to arm 1 before the treatment pause were instructed to continue their on-demand treatment during the pause. Patients who were randomized to arm 2 were instructed to discontinue concizumab treatment, and these patients received available standard of care treatment during the pause.
L438: In order to mitigate the risk for thromboembolic events the Applicant initiated a new guidance for treatment of mild and moderate breakthrough bleeding episodes with specific guidance for use of the lowest dose of rescue factor product or bypassing agent while on concizumab PPX. The Applicant also revised the concizumab dosing regimen, consisting of a loading dose of 1.0 mg/kg (unchanged from the initial dosing regimen), and an initial daily dose of 0.20 mg/kg concizumab (instead of 0.25 mg/kg). The Applicant also revised the criteria for an increase or decrease in the daily maintenance dose to 0.25 mg/kg or 0.15 mg/kg, respectively, based on concizumab exposure level at the Week 4 visit after initiating treatment. After the restart of the study, patients continued in the treatment arm to which they were initially randomized or assigned. Newly eligible patients were randomized to concizumab PPX (arm 2), no PPX (arm 1), or assigned to arm 4.
L439: The study visit schedule is shown in Figure 62 and the study schedule of events are shown in Table 119. Drug stopping criteria included clinical or laboratory evidence of thrombosis, microangiopathy, disseminated intravascular coagulopathy, or hypersensitivity reactions to concizumab.
L440: **Figure 7. Study 4311 Schema**
L441: Source: Study NN7415-4311 Clinical Study Report, Figure 9-1 (p.42)
L442: Note: the individual maintenance dose was either 0.15, 0.20 or 0.25 mg/kg concizumab.
L443: Abbreviations: OnD, on demand; PPX, prophylaxis; V, visit
L444: ## Concizumab Dosing
L445: Initially, patients randomized or allocated to the concizumab prophylaxis received a loading dose of 1.0 mg/kg concizumab SC on the first day of treatment, followed by a maintenance dose of
L446: 0.25 mg/kg concizumab given as a daily SC injection from the second day onwards. After the concizumab treatment pause and restart, the dosing regimen was as follows: When patients were randomized/allocated to concizumab prophylaxis they received a loading dose of 1.0 mg/kg concizumab at visit 2a (arm 2, 3 and 4) or visit 9a (arm 1) followed by an initial daily dose of 0.20 mg/kg concizumab from treatment Day 2. Within an initial 5 to 8-week maintenance dose setting period on 0.20 mg/kg concizumab, the patients could be increased or decreased in dose to 0.25 mg/kg or 0.15 mg/kg concizumab or remain at 0.20 mg/kg based on concizumab exposure level of &lt;200ng/mL, &gt;4000ng/mL or between 200-4000ng/mL, respectively. The study drug dosing schematic is shown in the figure below.
L447: **Figure 8. Concizumab Dosing**
L448: Source: Applicant figure Protocol 4311
L449: Notes: Concizumab exposure level is measured at V4a/9a.2; Potential maintenance dose setting will take place at V4a.1/9a.3.
L450: Abbreviations: LD, loading dose; PPX, prophylaxis; V, visit
L451: *Clinical Reviewer Comment: The open-label study design of comparing concizumab PPX to on demand treatment with bypassing agents is acceptable, as it would be unethical to include a placebo control arm. This approach is consistent with other drugs approved for hemophilia (e.g., emicizumab). See Section 7 for further discussion regarding the potential impact of the study pause on the study results.*
L452: ## 6.2.1.2. Eligibility Criteria, Study 4311
L453: The key inclusion criteria for Study 4311 are as follows:
L454: - Informed consent obtained before any study-related activities
- Male aged \ge 12 years at the time of signing informed consent
- Body weight &gt;25 kg at screening
- Congenital Hemophilia A or B of any severity with documented history of inhibitor (\ge 0.6 Bethesda Units)
- Patient has been prescribed, or needs treatment with bypassing agents in the last 24 weeks prior to screening (for patients not previously enrolled in NN7415-4310)
L455: The key exclusion criteria for Study 4311 are as follows:
L456: - Known or suspected hypersensitivity to any constituent of the study product or related products
L457: - Previous participation in this study due to pause. However, this is not applicable for patients who were screen failed at Applicant's decision due to the treatment pause
- Participation in any clinical study of an approved or non-approved investigational medicinal product within 5 half-lives or 30 days from screening, whichever is longer (not applicable for patients from Study 4310)
- Platelets \le 100,000/\mu\text{L} at screening
- Fibrinogen below laboratory lower normal limit at screening
- Hepatic dysfunction defined as serum aspartate aminotransferase (AST) and/or serum alanine aminotransferase (ALT) &gt;3 times the upper limit of normal (ULN), combined with serum total bilirubin (Tbili) &gt;1.5 times the ULN at screening.
- Renal impairment defined as estimated Glomerular Filtration Rate (eGFR) \le 30 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2 for serum creatinine measured at screening
- Known inherited or acquired coagulation disorder other than congenital hemophilia
- History of thromboembolic disease, current clinical signs of or treatment for thromboembolic disease, and patients who, in the judgement of the investigator, are considered at high risk of thromboembolic event
- A known systemic inflammatory condition requiring systemic treatment at screening
- Treatment with emicizumab within 180 days before screening
- Ongoing or planned Immune Tolerance Induction treatment
L458: ## 6.2.1.3. Endpoints, Study 4311
L459: ## Primary Endpoint
L460: The number of treated spontaneous and traumatic bleeding episodes:
L461: - On demand (arm 1): From randomization (Week 0) up until start of concizumab treatment (at least 24 weeks)
- Concizumab PPX (arm 2): From start of the new concizumab dosing regimen (Week 0) up until the primary analysis cut-off (at least 32 weeks)
L462: ## Key Secondary Endpoints
L463: - Change in SF-36v2 bodily pain, from start of treatment (Week 0) until Week 24
- Change in SF-36v2 physical functioning, from start of treatment (Week 0) until Week 24
L464: ## Other Efficacy Endpoints
L465: - Number of treated spontaneous bleeding episodes
L466: - Number of treated spontaneous and traumatic joint bleeds
- Number of treated spontaneous and traumatic target joint bleeds
L467: *Clinical Reviewer Comment:* While not prespecified as an endpoint the Applicant also described the number of patients with bleeding episodes. This endpoint is clinically meaningful, as having zero patients with bleeds is the ultimate goal to hemophilia prophylaxis.

- **Safety analysis set (SAS)**: All patients exposed to concizumab PPX or randomized to no PPX. All patients will contribute to the evaluation 'as treated'.

L485: Estimand Attribute	Content
Intercurrent event strategy	
Permanent treatment discontinuation. For this intercurrent event, the data from the period after permanent discontinuation of study treatment were not included. It is to be noted that for patients in the on-demand arm, permanent treatment discontinuation means initiation of PPX.
Temporary treatment discontinuation after restart of the study. For this intercurrent event the 'treatment policy' strategy was used meaning that the data from this period were included.
Use of factor products not related to treatment of a bleed. For this intercurrent event, the data during this period were not includeda.
Minor surgery. For this intercurrent event the 'treatment policy' strategy was used.

Population-level summary	The treatment ratio of the ABRs between the two treatment regimens
L486: Source: Study NN7415-4311 Clinical Study Report, Figure 8-2 (p.41)
L487: a Use of factor products for surgery and medical procedures was exempted
L488: Abbreviations: ABR, annualized bleeding rate; HAwI, hemophilia A with inhibitors; HBwI, hemophilia B with inhibitors; PPX, prophylaxis
L489: The comparison of the number of treated bleeds between the randomized arms 1 and 2 was based on the FAS and the on-treatment without ancillary therapy exclude data on initial regimen for patients exposed to both regimens (OtwoATexIR) analysis data set using negative binomial regression with the patient's number of bleeds analyzed as a function of the randomized treatment regimen, type of hemophilia (HAWI or HBWI) and bleeding frequency (&lt;9 or ≥9 bleeding episodes during the past 24 weeks prior to screening) and the logarithm of the length of the observation period included as an offset in the model.
L490: From the statistical model, an estimate of the rate ratio of the annualized bleeding rate (ABR) between the treatment regimens (concizumab prophylaxis and no prophylaxis) with corresponding 95% confidence interval and a p-value for the test for superiority were provided. Estimates of the actual ABRs with corresponding 95% confidence intervals were also provided for arms 1 and 2.
L491: ## Analysis of Key Secondary Endpoints
L492: The two key secondary endpoints included in the statistical testing hierarchy were:
L493: - Change in SF-36v2 bodily pain from start of treatment (Week 0) until Week 24
- Change in SF-36v2 physical functioning from start of treatment (Week 0) until Week 24
L494: The Week 0 is defined as time of randomization to on-demand administration or time of start of the new concizumab dosing regimen.
L495: The two key secondary endpoints based on SF-36v2 were analyzed including all patients in the FAS using the on-treatment without data on initial regimen (OtexIR) analysis dataset.
L496: Due to the low number of patients in arms 1 and 2 contributing data both at baseline and post-baseline, the pre-planned multiple imputation (MI) analyses for key secondary endpoint based on SF-36 could not be performed as planned due to non-convergence. Instead of using MI, a post hoc mixed model for repeated measurements (MMRM) was used in the following way:
L497: The changes in SF-36v2 scores from Week 0 to Weeks 4, 8, 16 and 24 were analyzed using a MMRM, with treatment, type of hemophilia (HAWI or HBWI) and bleeding frequency (&lt;9 or ≥9 bleeding episodes during the 24 weeks prior to screening) as factors, and baseline SF-36v2 as a covariate, all nested within week (Week 4, Week 8, Week 16 and Week 24). An unstructured covariance matrix was used to describe the variability for the repeated measurements for a patient. From the MMRM model, the treatment difference at Week 24 was estimated and the corresponding 95% confidence interval and p-value were presented. Change from baseline estimate and 95% CI for each arm was also obtained, in addition to the estimated mean score at Week 24 and corresponding 95% CI for each arm.
L498: *Statistical Reviewer's Comments: Because of the low number of patients in arms 1 and 2 and the following two reasons, the program for the MI analysis could not converge:*
L499: - *Large amount of the imputed values lied outside the range with unrealistic imputed values, (e.g., lower than -200 or higher than 300) which exceed the range of allowed values for the given PROs*
- *Not enough observations to fit regression models with a monotone regression method.*
L500: *The Applicant discussed the issue with the Agency during the pre-BLA meeting and proposed to use MMRM as an alternative model. The proposed alternative model was determined to be reasonable. However, the PRO endpoints were conducted in open-label studies, thus the Applicant's results should be interpreted with caution. As discussed in the FDA guidance for industry Patient-Reported Outcome Measures: Use in Medical Product Development to Support Labeling Claims1, "Open-label clinical studies, where patients and investigators are aware of assigned therapy, are rarely adequate to support labeling claims based on PRO instruments. Patients who know they are in an active treatment group may overestimate benefit whereas patients who know they are not receiving active treatment may under-report the improvement they experienced."*
L501: ## **Multiple Testing Strategy**
L502: The type I error was controlled at 5% significance level (two-sided) using a hierarchical (fixed sequence) testing procedure. This was based on a priority ordering of the null hypotheses for major efficacy endpoints. If a hypothesis was rejected (superiority confirmed), the significance level was to be reallocated to the next hypothesis in the hierarchy and then that hypothesis was to be tested at the 5% significance level. This process was repeated sequentially until no further hypotheses can be confirmed.
L503: The steps in the hierarchical testing procedure were as follows:
L504: - Step 1: Superiority in number of treated spontaneous and traumatic bleeding episodes
- Step 2: Superiority in change from baseline in SF-36v2 bodily pain
- Step 3: Superiority in change from baseline in SF-36v2 physical functioning
L505: 1 https://www.fda.gov/regulatory-information/search-fda-guidance-documents/patient-reported-outcome-measures-use-medical-product-development-support-labeling-claims
L506: ## Handling of Intercurrent Event or Missing Data
L507: - The primary endpoint: refer to intercurrent event strategy in Table 10.
- Key secondary endpoints: MMRM method handles missing data under the missing at random (MAR) assumption

L547: While more patients in arm 1 (no PPX) had high titer inhibitors levels at screening compared to arm 2 (concizumab PPX), the patient population remains consistent with patients with HAwI and HBwI as all patients had a documented history of an inhibitor level &gt;0.6BU. Given the mechanism of action of concizumab, which is not impacted by FVIII or FIX, it is not expected that the titer of the inhibitor would impact the efficacy results.
L548: The historical bleeding rate of each patient enrolled was reviewed by investigators and only documented as above or below 9 bleeds in the previous 24 weeks. The actual number of bleeds was not recorded in datasets so the baseline bleeding rate could not be reported for each patient. While not ideal, the study results are still interpretable as the Applicant did stratify randomization for above or below 9 bleeds in the previous 24 weeks therefore, the severity of hemophilia based on the previous 24 weeks is expected to be similar between study arms.
L549: ## **Analysis of the Primary Endpoint, Study 4311**
L550: Superiority of concizumab PPX (arm 2) over no PPX (arm 1) was confirmed for the primary endpoint number of treated spontaneous and traumatic bleeding episodes. Using a negative binomial regression model, the estimated mean ABR was 1.7 with 95% CI (1.01, 2.87) for patients on concizumab PPX (arm 2) and 11.8 with 95% CI (7.03, 19.86) for patients on no PPX (arm 1). The estimated ABR ratio between patients on concizumab PPX (arm 2) and no PPX (arm 1) was 0.14 with 95% CI (0.07, 0.29) and 2-sided p-value&lt;0.001, corresponding to an 86% reduction in ABR for patients on concizumab PPX (arm 2) compared to no PPX (arm 1).
L551: The Applicant's primary efficacy analysis results were confirmed by the FDA statistical reviewer. Results of the primary efficacy endpoint are summarized in Table 16.
L552: Descriptive results (e.g., median, IQR, mean, standard deviation (SD), min, max, etc.) for bleeding episodes related endpoints were also provided by the Applicant for arm 1 to 4 and summarized in Supplementary Section 16.1.

L559: *Statistical Reviewer's Comments: Study 4311 had a concizumab treatment pause for 6 months during the study due to thromboembolic events reported in 3 patients. After the concizumab treatment pause and subsequent restart of Study 4311, a new concizumab dosing regimen was introduced to concizumab PPX arms. The original randomization was potentially compromised because more than half of the planned sample size had already been enrolled and treated on the original regimen. There were 4 patients randomized before pause in concizumab PPX arm 2 who did not restart on the new concizumab dosing regimen. Please refer to Section 8.5.1 for further clinical and statistical evaluation and discussion regarding the determination if the pause in Study 4311 due to thrombotic events impacted the benefit (ABR).*
L560: ## Analysis of Key Secondary Endpoints, Study 4311
L561: Because the primary efficacy endpoint analysis was statistically significant (2-sided p-value&lt;0.001) in favor of concizumab PPX, the two key secondary efficacy endpoints were further tested in the prespecified hierarchical order.
L562: For both key secondary endpoints, the low number of patients in arms 1 and 2 contributing data at both baseline and post-baseline caused a convergence issue in the pre-planned multiple imputation (MI) analyses. Instead of using MI, the Applicant conducted post hoc mixed model for repeated measurements (MMRM) for both key secondary endpoints.
L563: ## SF-36v2 Bodily Pain
L564: The first key secondary endpoint was change in SF-36 v2 bodily pain from Week 0 to Week 24. Applying a mixed model for repeated measurements (MMRM), the estimated mean change in the bodily pain score from baseline to Week 24 was 9.2 with 95% CI (5.06, 13.25) for patients on concizumab PPX (arm 2) compared to 2.2 with 95% CI (-5.14, 9.52) for patients on no PPX (arm 1), yielding a difference estimate at Week 24 of 6.96 with 95% CI (-1.64, 15.57) and 2-sided p-value=0.109. This endpoint failed to achieve statistically significant superiority in SF-36v2 bodily pain score change from baseline to Week 24 for concizumab PPX compared to no PPX. The statistical testing hierarchy for key secondary endpoints was stopped at the endpoint SF-36v2 bodily pain and the results of the endpoints after it can only be considered descriptive. The Applicant's primary efficacy analysis results were confirmed by the FDA statistical reviewer and summarized in Table 17.

L578: *Statistical Reviewer's Comments: This post hoc analysis using MMRM model has similar underlying assumptions to the initially planned multiple imputation (MI) model (i.e., Missing at Random), which can be a reasonable alternative model now that the pre-planned MI model cannot converge due to the low number of patients in arms 1 and 2 contributing data both at baseline and post-baseline. However, unlike the findings for the primary endpoint, both key secondary endpoints failed to achieve significant result using the MMRM model.*
L579: ## **Subgroup Analysis of the Primary Endpoints, Study 4311**
L580: Subgroup analyses were conducted to assess potential differences in the treatment effect for various demographic and clinical characteristics groups. Because of the small size of the study, only descriptive statistics for the randomized arm 1 (no PPX) and arm 2 (concizumab PPX) by age, race, ethnicity, region, and hemophilia subtype were summarized by the FDA statistical reviewer in Table 19. The sample size was imbalanced for different categories of subgroups and many subgroup analysis results were limited due to very small numbers of patients. Therefore, the subgroup analysis Results should be interpreted with caution. For those categories with relatively larger sample size, results were generally consistent with the full analysis data set results.
L581: ## **Exploratory Endpoint, Study 4311**
L582: The Applicant described the number of patients with treated spontaneous and traumatic bleeding episodes in the first 24 weeks of treatment. For patients on no PPX (arm 1), 17 patients (89.5%) reported at least one bleeding event during the first 24 weeks, compared to 12 patients (36.4%) on concizumab PPX (arm 2). In total, 94.7% of patients in arm 1 (no PPX) had a treated bleed or discontinued before Week 24 compared to 48.5% in arm 2 (concizumab PPX).
L583: *Clinical Reviewer Comment: A numerically lower proportion of patients in the concizumab PPX arm experienced at least one bleeding episode in the main treatment period compared to no concizumab PPX. While clinically meaningful, these results should be interpreted with caution as this endpoint was not prespecified.*

L596: ## 6.2.2. Study 4310
L597: ## 6.2.2.1. Design, Study 4310
L598: Study 4310, EXPLORER 4, is titled, “A Multi-Centre, Randomized, Open-Label, Controlled Trial Evaluating the Efficacy and Safety of Prophylactic Administration of Concizumab in Hemophilia A and B Patients with Inhibitors.” This study is considered supportive of the safety and efficacy profile of concizumab for the treatment of patients with HAwI and HBwI.
L599: Study 4310 is a Phase 2 interventional, multi-national, multicenter, randomized (2:1), open-label, controlled, dose escalation study of concizumab 0.15 mg/kg, 0.20 mg/kg and 0.25 mg/kg administered daily SC compared to treatment with eptacog alfa (rFVIIa) in patients with HAwI and HBwI. Only patients using on-demand therapies were eligible for the study. The study consisted of a 2-week screening period, a subsequent 76 to 118-week treatment period and an 8-week follow-up period. The 76 to 118-week treatment period consisted of a main part which lasted 24 weeks for patients randomized to eptacog alfa and at least 24 weeks for patients randomized to concizumab. The extension part lasted up to 94 weeks. The study schema is shown in the figure below.
L600: In all patients treated with concizumab, a single 90\mug/kg dose of eptacog alfa was administered in a non-bleeding state one week after dosing with concizumab had been initiated. The investigator evaluated if there were any safety concerns 24 hours post eptacog alfa administration. Furthermore, the scheduled administration of eptacog alfa was staggered for the first four patients entering the study with concizumab. In the comparator arm, patients received eptacog alfa on-demand treatment during the main part of the study. After completion of the main part, the patients continued the study in the extension part being treated with prophylactic daily SC doses of 0.15 mg/kg concizumab (with potential dose escalation).
L601: Figure 10. Trial Schema, Study 4310
L602: Source: Applicant CSR for Study 4310
L603: Abbreviation: CTR, clinical trial report; rFVIIa, recombinant activated factor VII; V, visit
L604: ## 6.2.2.2. Eligibility Criteria, Study 4310
L605: ## Key Inclusion Criteria
L606: - Informed consent obtained before any study related activities
- Male HA or HB patients with inhibitors aged \ge 18 years at the time of signing informed consent
- Patients currently in need of treatment with bypassing agents
L607: ## Key Exclusion Criteria
L608: - Known or suspected hypersensitivity to study product(s) or related products
- Known inherited or acquired bleeding disorder other than hemophilia
- Ongoing or planned immune tolerance induction therapy or prophylaxis with FVIII or FIX
L609: ## 6.2.2.3. Statistical Analysis Plan, Study 4310
L610: Key information regarding statistical analysis from the clinical study report, protocol, and statistical analysis plan (SAP) are summarized in this section.
L611: ## Analysis Population
L612: All dosed patients were included in the Full Analysis Set (FAS) as well as in Safety Analysis Set (SAS).
L613: *Statistical Reviewer's Comments: According to the Study 4310 Clinical Study Report (CSR), all the 26 subjects randomized to this study were dosed. Thus, the FAS is the same as the Intent-to-Treat, which is defined as all randomized patients.*
L614: ## Analysis of the Primary Endpoint
L615: The primary endpoint for this study was the number of bleeding episodes occurring at least 24 weeks from treatment onset. All treated bleeding episodes were considered for this endpoint, including bleeding episodes recorded as post-surgical or caused by surgery or other medical or dental procedures. The reporting of the primary endpoint was produced via two Clinical Proof of Concept (CpoC) criteria that were evaluated in a hierarchical fashion:
L616: - The primary CpoC (CpoC1) criterion aimed at evaluating the effect of concizumab at the last dose level reached for a patient. Hence, for this evaluation, only observations from the period where patients were on their last dose level at the time of analysis contributed to the analysis.
- The secondary CpoC (CpoC2) criterion aimed at evaluating the effect of concizumab when given as an escalation regimen. This analysis included all bleeding episodes (i.e., from the entire treatment period).
L617: The primary endpoint was analyzed based on the FAS using a negative binomial regression with log of exposure time in the included observational period of the main part as offset and regimen as factor.
L618: - The offset for first CpoC criterion of patients in concizumab arm is the log of the individual exposure time at the last dose level reached at the time of analysis excluding the 2 weeks run-in period for patients on 0.15 mg/kg
- The offset for the second criterion of patients in concizumab arm is the log of the individual exposure time in the main part
- For patients in the on-demand arm regardless of criterion, the offset is the log of the exposure time in the main part
L619: The analysis provides an estimate of the ABR ratio between regimens (concizumab prophylactic and on-demand eptacog alfa (rFVIIa)) with corresponding 95% confidence interval in addition to the actual estimate of the ABR with corresponding 95% confidence interval for each regimen.
L620: ## **Multiple Testing Strategy**
L621: Two CpoC criteria were evaluated in a hierarchical fashion following the aforementioned order. No secondary endpoints were included in the statistical testing hierarchy.
L622: ## **Handling of Intercurrent Event or Missing Data**
L623: The primary endpoint: The primary analysis method had the underlying assumption that the missing data mechanism is “missing at random”, (i.e., MAR). Only data collected prior to discontinuation of study product or initiation of alternative treatment options were used to draw inference.
L624: ## 6.2.2.4. Results of Analyses, Study 4310
L625: ## **Patient Disposition**
L626: Overall, in Study 4310 there were 26 patients randomized, 17 patients (HAWI = 9, HBWI = 8) randomized to concizumab and 9 patients (HAWI = 7, HBWI = 2) randomized to eptacog alfa. The table below shows the patient disposition in Study 4310. A total of 25 patients completed the main part and were exposed to concizumab in the extension part. Of the 25 patients, 22 (88%) patients completed the treatment, and 3 patients discontinued the treatment, 2 were discontinued due to ‘other’ reasons (1 was due to withdrawal of consent, 1 was due to suspect of no therapeutic effect due to normal TFPI level with binding antibody) and 1 was due to lack of efficacy (more than 3 treated spontaneous bleeding episodes within 12 weeks on 0.25 mg/kg concizumab).

L645: ## Analysis of the Primary Endpoint, Study 4310
L646: ## **The Primary CpoC (CpoC1) Criterion**
L647: The primary CpoC (CpoC1) criterion aimed at evaluating the effect of concizumab at the last dose level reached for a patient. Hence, for this evaluation, only observations from the period when patients were on their last dose level at the time of analysis contributed to the analysis.
L648: In the concizumab arm, the estimated ABR on the last dose level for each patient was 4.5 with a 95% CI of [3.2, 6.4]. In the eptacog alfa (rFVIIa) on-demand arm, the estimated ABR was 20.4 with a 95% CI of [14.4, 29.1]. The ABR ratio was 0.22 with a 95% CI of [0.13, 0.36]. The analysis results are summarized in Table 23.

L666: *Statistical Reviewer's Comments: In Study 4310, the primary endpoint of number of bleeding episodes showed a significant result in which ABR was lower in the concizumab arm compared to the Eptacog alfa arm, which may serve as supportive evidence for the proposed indication. However, Study 4310 was a very small sized, open-label Phase 2 study, therefore the results of the study should be interpreted with caution.*
L667: Dedicated juvenile animal studies have not been conducted with concizumab as studies in neonatal and infant monkeys are technically challenging and are unlikely to identify potential reactions that were not identified from studies in fully developed (adult) animals. Notable findings in the hemostatic system and immune-related vascular toxicity were observed in adolescent (2 to 3 years of age) and sexually mature (3.5 to 6 years of age) cynomolgus monkeys. The human hemostatic system matures during the early weeks and months of life, and the concentrations of most hemostatic proteins, both in full-term and preterm infants, are comparable to adult values by 6 months of age (Albisetti 2003; Chalmers 2004).
L668: **Table 25. Nonclinical Data Supporting Labeling on Pregnancy and Lactation**
L669: Labeling Section	Nonclinical Data
Pregnancy	
Dedicated reproductive and developmental toxicology studies were not conducted as concizumab is primarily indicated for use in male patients with hemophilia and the clinical development program was conducted exclusively in males.
Hemophilia A is inherited in an X-linked recessive manner and the disease which occurs predominantly in males (99%) is extremely rare in females. Concizumab is also being indicated for hemophilia B (Factor IX deficiency or Christmas disease, caused by mutations in the Factor IX (FIX) gene on the X chromosome) and hence, is predominantly observed in males (some females who carry the gene may display mild or, rarely, severe symptoms of bleeding).

8.3 Females and Males of Reproductive Potential	
Histopathological evaluations conducted during repeat-dose toxicology studies in adolescent (up to 13 weeks in duration) and sexually mature (up to 52 weeks in duration) male and female cynomolgus monkeys did not elicit any notable effects in reproductive organs.
In mice, inactivation of the TFPI-gene has been shown to result in embryonic lethality (Huang et al. 1997; Ellery et al. 2015) and total TFPI deficiency has not been described in humans (Wood et al. 2014). As placental transfer of IgG, particularly during organogenesis, may differ between mice and humans, the clinical relevance of these findings is unknown (DeSesso et al. 2012).
Recurrent pregnancy loss has been observed in women who exhibited TFPI deficiency in combination with an Activated Protein C (APC) resistance phenotype or in women with anti-TFPI antibodies in combination with antiphospholipid antibodies and suggests that TFPI may be critical to the maintenance of normal pregnancy in humans (Martinuzzo et al. 2005; Gardiner et al. 2006).
The potential risk of blocking TFPI with a monoclonal IgG antibody (e.g., concizumab) in pregnant women is not clear.
L670: 13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility	
Concizumab is not functional in rodents and standard carcinogenicity studies have not been conducted. Instead, the Applicant conducted a carcinogenicity risk assessment of concizumab using a weight-of-evidence (WOE) approach that considered the mode of action (MOA), a review of relevant literature and an evaluation of the nonclinical and clinical data. It was concluded that treatment with concizumab has the potential to enhance the prothrombotic conditions observed in some cancer patients (as would be expected for any procoagulant compound). However, the assessment did not identify evidence that treatment with concizumab could definitively lead to an increased risk of inducing or enhancing tumor development in humans. The Division determined that no additional nonclinical studies assessing the carcinogenic potential of concizumab were warranted for the indications of hemophilia A and hemophilia B and the CDER Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee (ECAC) concurred with the Division's recommendation.
Genotoxicity studies were not conducted, as concizumab consists of naturally occurring amino acids (endogenous) and is not anticipated to be genotoxic and there were no mechanistic data that suggested a mutagenic potential for concizumab.
Fertility evaluations were included in the 26-week chronic toxicity study conducted in sexually mature male and female cynomolgus monkeys and there were no notable effects on the menstrual cycle duration, the length, width and volume of the testis, total sperm count, sperm motility or sperm morphology in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab at doses up to 9 mg/kg/day (SC).
L671: Source: Reviewer constructed summary table of reproductive toxicology studies submitted to the BLA.
L672: Abbreviations: APC, Activated Protein C; ECAC, Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee; FIX, Factor IX; IgG, immunoglobulin G; MOA, mode of action; SC, subcutaneous; TFPI, tissue factor pathway inhibitor; WOE, weight-of-evidence.

L678: ## 6.3. Key Efficacy Review Issues
L679: ## 6.3.1. Determination If the Pause in Study 4311 Due to Thrombotic Events Impacted the Benefit (ABR)
L680: ## Issue
L681: Determination if the pause in Study 4311 due to thrombotic events impacted the benefit (ABR).
L682: ## Background
L683: Study 4311 had a concizumab treatment pause for 6 months due to thromboembolic events reported in 3 patients. After the concizumab treatment pause and subsequent restart of Study 4311, a new concizumab dosing regimen was introduced to concizumab PPX arms. The original randomization could have been compromised because more than half of the planned sample size (refer to Table 27) were already enrolled and treated on the original regimen. Four patients randomized before the study pause in the concizumab PPX arm 2 did not restart on the new concizumab dosing regimen.

L687: ## Assessment
L688: As recommended in previous IND meetings, the impact of potential bias introduced by the treatment pause and the robustness of the primary analysis was investigated by the Applicant with four sensitivity analyses. The results from all four sensitivity analyses in Table 28 were confirmed by the FDA statistical reviewer. It was determined that the results of superiority from the primary analysis are robust.
L689: **Table 28. Sensitivity Analysis for the Primary Efficacy Endpoint: Number of Treated Spontaneous and Traumatic Bleeding Episodes, Study 4311**
L690: Purpose	Sensitivity Analysis	Result
To investigate the robustness of directly using the bleed data on the initial dosing regimen for patients who were not exposed to the new concizumab regimen	Used multiple imputation to impute the number of treated bleeds on the new concizumab dosing regimen for the 4 patients in arm 2 who did not restart	Estimated ABR ratio (arm 2/arm 1) was 0.14 with 95% CI (0.07, 0.29) and 2-sided p-value <0.001
To investigate the effect of using bleed data on the initial dosing regimen on the treatment effect of concizumab	Used all concizumab exposure data in arm 2 up until the primary analysis cut-off, irrespective of the dosing regimen	Estimated ABR ratio (arm 2/arm 1) was 0.14 with 95% CI (0.07, 0.29) and 2-sided p-value <0.001
To investigate the robustness of a potential superiority conclusion in terms of missing information for patients who were not exposed to the new concizumab regimen	Conducted tipping point analysis for patients only exposed to the initial dosing regimen. Patients only exposed to the initial dosing regimen were modelled with an increasing bleeding rate until the conclusion of superiority is changed	Tipping point was 25, means patients only exposed to the initial dosing regimen need to have 25 more bleed episodes to have non-significant result, which is considered implausible
To investigate a potential difference in treatment effects between patients randomized before and after the pause	Conducted subgroup analysis for patients randomized before and after treatment pause	Estimated ABR ratio (arm 2/arm 1) for patients randomized before the pause was 0.16 with 95% CI (0.08, 0.35)
Estimated ABR ratio (arm 2/arm 1) for patients randomized after restart was 0.06 with 95% CI (0.01, 0.42)
L691: Source: Study NN7415-4311 Clinical Study Report, Section 9.7.1.2.2 and section 11.2.2; FDA statistical reviewer's analysis.
Abbreviations: ABR, annualized bleeding rate; CI, confidence interval.
L692: ## Conclusion
L693: Study 4311 was paused due to thromboembolic events however, multiple sensitivity analysis confirmed the conclusion of superiority of concizumab PPX over on-demand therapy in reduction in ABR. Despite the study pause, Study 4311 is determined to be an adequate and well controlled study and provides substantial evidence of effectiveness of concizumab for routine prophylaxis to prevent or reduce the frequency of bleeding episodes in adult and adolescent patients with HAwI and HBwI.
L694: ## 7. Safety (Risk and Risk Management)
L695: ## 7.1. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Based on Nonclinical Data
L696: Toxicological characterization of concizumab was assessed in cynomolgus monkeys (4 weeks, 13 weeks, 26 weeks, and 52 weeks) with normal intact coagulation systems (non-hemophilic) during repeat-dose toxicology studies. Cynomolgus monkeys were considered the most relevant
L697: nonclinical species for toxicological evaluations based on amino acid sequence homology between humans and cynomolgus monkeys in the target epitope (K2 domain), and the observed binding and functional cross-reactivity of concizumab to human and cynomolgus monkey endogenous TFPI (Section 13.1).
L698: No definitive risks were identified during in vitro safety pharmacology assessments (human Fc receptor binding, antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC), complement fixation and binding to peripheral blood cells) or during in vivo safety pharmacology assessments conducted as part of the 13-week and/or 52-week repeat-dose toxicity studies in cynomolgus monkeys (cytokine release, blood pressure measurement, ECG, respiration rate, urinalysis, CNS endpoints). Furthermore, activated protein C (APC) generation and antithrombin activity (negative regulators of the coagulation cascade) were not affected by the presence of concizumab (in vitro), and these results were consistent with the absence of changes in APC and antithrombin levels in the repeat-dose toxicology studies conducted in cynomolgus monkeys. The in vitro and in vivo safety pharmacology studies are discussed in further detail under Section 13.1.1.2.
L699: The results of the nonclinical toxicology studies were consistent with the expected mechanism of action of TFPI inhibition, which results in a downstream increase in thrombin generation and hemostasis. These events were reflected by an increase in markers of coagulation activation, Thrombin-Antithrombin (TAT) and D-dimers, and a decrease in fibrinogen levels in repeat-dose cynomolgus monkey studies. Minor changes in clotting parameters (prolonged APTT and PT and/or a decrease in platelet counts) were induced at supra-pharmacological exposures that exceeded 400-fold the anticipated exposure at the proposed therapeutic dose during the 26-/52-week toxicity studies. This response is likely indicative of homeostatic regulation of the coagulation system in cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state.
L700: Notable findings in nonclinical toxicology studies conducted in cynomolgus monkeys included thrombi and focal vascular changes. Thrombi were predominantly observed in the lungs and heart and were associated with a single early mortality (HD female presented with lung thrombi, lung parenchymal necrosis, and anemia in the 26-week study) across all of the repeat-dose toxicology studies conducted in cynomolgus monkeys. The onset of thrombi was regarded as an exaggerated pharmacological effect of administering a procoagulant to cynomolgus monkeys with normal intact coagulation systems. Focal vascular changes were primarily observed in the choroid plexus (in the cerebral ventricles of the brain), lungs, and at the injection sites and included endothelial hypertrophy, subendothelial thickening and/or inflammatory cell infiltration. Mechanistic analyses (discussed in detail under Section 13.1.2.5.3) suggested that the observed vascular changes were due to an immunogenic reaction driven by the development of anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) and subsequent formation/deposition of immune-complexes (Ics). The extent of ADA-related-immune complex formation exceeded the clearance capacity for Ics in cynomolgus monkeys and the associated vascular changes were found to be reversible, consistent with the eventual clearance of the Ics. There were no notable changes observed in immunotoxicity, local tolerance, or fertility endpoints incorporated into the repeat-dose toxicity studies.
L701: The toxicities observed in nonclinical studies occurred at exposures which were significantly higher than those anticipated at the proposed therapeutic dose and interpretation of these safety margins should also take into consideration that the toxicology studies were conducted in normal, healthy cynomolgus monkeys with intact coagulation systems. This clearly shifted the
L702: toxicological exposure/response curve leftward, and the exaggerated pharmacological effects and immunogenic responses observed in cynomolgus monkeys were not recapitulated in clinical studies (albeit at significantly lower exposures). Thus, the results from the toxicology studies more accurately represent the consequences of excessive procoagulant effects in normal (non-disease state) animals and, presumably, what may occur in human subjects who do not harbor a clotting disorder, such as hemophilia. Additionally, patients with hemophilia would be expected to have a reduced immunogenic response towards a humanized mAb like concizumab, and the risk of IC formation exceeding the clearance capacity in humans is considered low. The repeat-dose toxicity studies in cynomolgus monkeys are discussed in further detail under Section 13.1.2.

L708: ## 7.2. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Based on Drug Class or Other Drug-Specific Factors
L709: Concizumab elicited a minimal release of TFPI from the surface of endothelial cells (compared to heparin) and is consistent with the mechanism required for TFPI-release that involves association with the C-terminal region (not the K2 domain) of the TFPI\alpha form. This result suggests that the potential risk of concizumab-mediated release of TFPI from the surface endothelial cells (an anticoagulant effect) is low.
L710: In certain pathological situations (e.g., severe generalized infections and/or sepsis) increased expression of tissue factor (TF) can occur. Because TFPI is involved in regulation of the TF-related response, it is a theoretical concern that inhibition of TFPI in situations with increased TF expression may lead to increased thrombogenicity, and thereby complicate and/or aggravate these clinical conditions. Situations with increased expression of TF have not been specifically investigated in nonclinical studies conducted with concizumab, and based on the literature, a theoretical concern of thrombotic events cannot be excluded if concizumab is used in patients with increased TF expression.
L711: ## Issue
L712: PK fluctuations leading to subtherapeutic concentrations during concizumab treatment
L713: ## **Background**
L714: As stated earlier, concizumab's clearance through TMDD is predominant in the target exposure of 200 to 4000 ng/mL. The effective half-life in the target exposure range is estimated to be only one to two days which necessitates once-daily dosing of concizumab to reduce peak-to-trough fluctuations. Additionally, due to variability in target pool (membrane bound TFPI) between patients, PK guided dosing is necessary to maintain concizumab exposures within the target range. Despite these measures, significant fluctuations in plasma PK profiles were observed in individual patients.
L715: In Study 4311, PK samples for measuring concizumab concentrations were collected at each study visit throughout the study, which allows for review of the observed PK profile over the course of concizumab treatment in each individual patient (Figure 11). For example, patient ID [REDACTED](b)(6) had an 87-fold higher concizumab concentration at Week 12 compared to Week 8 at the dose of 0.2 mg/kg. Following the dose increase to 0.25 mg/kg between Week 12 and Week 16, the concizumab concentrations at Week 20 was 10500 ng/mL but decreased to 970 ng/mL (approximately 90% decrease) at Week 24, while the dose was not changed. In another example, patient ID [REDACTED](b)(6) exhibited an increase in concizumab concentration to 1200 ng/mL after the dose increase to 0.25 mg/kg, but during subsequent visits the levels decreased to 5 ng/mL (99% decrease in exposure) while on the same maintenance dose. These patients PK profiles prompted the review team to look systematically into patients whose concentrations during concizumab treatment either went below or exceeded the target exposure range. The review team noted that 42 (43%) out of 97 patients had concizumab exposure levels below 200 ng/mL at some point during the maintenance dose setting and up until primary analysis cut-off (PACO). Upon further review, the team identified a small proportion of patients (e.g., patient ID [REDACTED](b)(6)) who had concizumab concentrations far below the therapeutic range for a prolonged period.
L716: It should be noted that the lower end of the target exposure range for efficacy i.e., 200 ng/mL, is adequately supported by exposure-response (E-R) and PK/PD data from Study 4311 (See Section 6.1). An increased risk for bleeding is expected when concentrations fall below 200 ng/mL. That said, concizumab exposures occasionally dropping below 200 ng/mL probably does not pose a significant risk, as the previously identified 42 patients have ABR that was significantly lower than in patients without concizumab treatment. However, it is the few patients whose exposure falls below 200 ng/mL for prolonged periods that is of concern. This is reflected in patient ID [REDACTED](b)(6), who had concizumab concentrations near or below the subtherapeutic levels for prolonged periods before the fatal bleeding event (intracranial hemorrhage). A 100-fold change in concizumab levels were observed in Patient ID [REDACTED](b)(6), where the trough concentration was about 3000 ng/mL at Week 24 when concizumab was dosed at the study site and 28 ng/mL at Week 48, 8 days before the fatal bleeding event occurred (See Table 30). Therefore, the review team is mainly concerned that consistently lower concentrations of concizumab far below 200 ng/mL for a prolonged period of time may put the patient at risk for bleeding, sometimes even a serious/fatal bleeding event.
L717: Figure 11. Pre-dose Trough Concizumab Plasma Concentration (ng/mL) – Patients Ending on Maintenance Dose 0.20 mg/kg (Top Panels and Left Bottom Panel) and 0.25 mg/kg (Right Bottom Panel) – Individual Plots – HAwI + HBwI – OteXI R – Safety Analysis Set of Study 4311
L718: Source: Applicant's response to the FDA's Information Request on March 2nd, 2023

Treated bleeding episodes from OtwoATexIR are included as vertical markers at the bottom of each plot.
