L1187: ## 13. Pharmacology Toxicology
L1188: ## 13.1. Summary Review of Studies Submitted With the Investigational New Drug Application
L1189: ## 13.1.1. Concizumab Pharmacology
L1190: Concizumab binds to an epitope in the K2 domain of tissue factor pathway inhibitor (TFPI) that overlaps with the interface of Fxa binding. The results of in vitro and in vivo nonclinical pharmacology studies are consistent with the proposed mechanism of action of TFPI neutralization, whereby the Fxa produced by the activated coagulation factor VII (FVIIa)/tissue factor (TF) complex (normally downregulated by TFPI activity) results in increased thrombin generation leading to increased clot formation and theoretically hemostasis.
L1191: The rabbit and cynomolgus monkey were identified as relevant nonclinical species. No other nonclinical species were identified that expressed Kunitz domain sequences that contained the amino acid residues in the epitope of concizumab on the human K2 domain. The cynomolgus monkey and rabbit K2 domains have a 96% and 93% sequence homology to the human K2 domain, respectively.
L1192: The binding constants (K_D) of concizumab to rabbit, cynomolgus monkey and human TFPI are 0.22nM, 0.06nM, and 0.04nM, respectively (determined by surface plasmon resonance, SPR). The TFPI association constants (k_a) for concizumab were 1.3 \times 10^6, 4.2 \times 10^6, and 1.7 \times 10^6 (M^{-1} \times s^{-1}) and the TFPI disassociation constants (k_d) for concizumab were 2.8 \times 10^6, 2.3 \times 10^6, and 6.3 \times 10^6 (s^{-1}) in rabbits, cynomolgus monkeys and humans, respectively. These data suggest that concizumab binds to TFPI with high affinity and to a comparable extent in humans, cynomolgus monkeys and rabbits. Functional cross-reactivity was evaluated in a modified prothrombin time (PT) clot assay and concizumab was found to be functionally active against TFPI in plasmas derived from rabbits, cynomolgus monkeys, healthy humans, and FVIII-deficient humans (half maximal effective concentration (EC_{50}) values were 0.49nM, 0.41nM, 0.56nM, and 0.62nM, respectively).
L1193: Other names used for concizumab in this review include: NNC0172-2021, NNC0172-0000-2021, mAb 2021 and HzTFPI4F36.
L1194: ## 13.1.1.1. In Vitro, Ex Vivo, and In Vivo Pharmacodynamic Activity
L1195: **Table 57. Pharmacodynamic Studies**
L1196: Study Details	Findings
Study No: 209161
Study Title: Epitope Mapping of the Murine Anti-TFPI Antibody TFPI4F36 by X-ray crystallography
Species: Recombinantly produced K2 subdomain of human TFPI and the Fab fragment of murine TFPI4F36 were co-crystallized and the structure of the complex was solved by X-ray crystallography.
Control: N/A	The murine TFPI4F36 and concizumab antibodies share the same K2 binding mode of action and the complementarity determining region (CDR) loops governing the binding to the K2 region of TFPI were reported to be identical between TFPI4F36 and concizumab. Barring the K2 domains of TFPI in rabbits, monkeys (cynomolgus, orangutang and rhesus) and humans, no additional Kunitz domain epitopes were identified for TFPI4F36 and concizumab.
The epitope (binding surface on K2) and the paratope (binding surface on the antibody) are identical for the murine and the humanized antibodies and suggests that data derived with the murine antibody in this study is applicable to concizumab.
These results support the premise that the rabbit and the cynomolgus monkey are relevant nonclinical species. The binding epitope for TFPI4F36 to the second Kunitz domain (K2) exhibits an extensive overlap with the Factor Xa binding surface on K2, accounting for (on a structural basis) the neutralizing action of TFPI4F36 (and by extension concizumab) on TFPI.
L1197: Study Details	Findings
Study No: 210179
Study Title: Binding Affinity of Concizumab (NNC 0172-2021) for Recombinant TFPI derived from Human, Cynomolgus Monkey and Rabbit Protein Sequences assessed using a CM5 chip (carrying a matrix of carboxymethylated dextran covalently attached to a gold surface).
Species: Recombinant TFPI proteins derived from rabbits, monkeys, and human protein sequences.
Control: Assay Buffer	The binding constants (K_D) for recombinant TFPI were 0.04nM (human), 0.06nM (monkey) and 0.22nM (rabbits) and were considered comparable. This data are consistent with the findings that the epitope of the human Kunitz 2 domain (mapped by X-ray crystallography) is also found with TFPI derived from cynomolgus monkeys and rabbits.
Based on the binding affinities to recombinant TFPI, the rabbit and cynomolgus monkey are considered relevant species for the nonclinical assessment of concizumab.
Study No: 209168
Study Title: Neutralization of Plasma TFPI by Concizumab (HzTFPI4F36) or its Murine Precursor (MuTFPI4F36) in an In Vitro Dilute Prothrombin Time (dPT) Assay conducted with Plasma derived from Humans (Healthy or FVIII-Deficient) or Various Nonclinical Animal Species.
Species: Mouse, Rat, Guinea Pig, Rabbit (NZW), Dog, Cynomolgus Monkey, Pig, and Human.
Concentrations: 0.02nM to 20nM
Control: Plasma spiked with either buffer or a goat polyclonal anti-TFPI antibody or recombinant FVIIa.	Concizumab is capable of neutralizing endogenous TFPI (shortening dilute prothrombin time (dPT) clotting times) in plasma derived from humans (healthy or FVIII-deficient), cynomolgus monkeys and rabbits with similar potency.
The murine precursor of concizumab (MuTFPI4F36) elicited no effect on dPT in mouse, rat, dog, and pig plasma, and concizumab had no effect on dPT in guinea pig plasma.
These results support the premise that the rabbit and cynomolgus monkey are relevant nonclinical species.
Study No.: 209169
Study Title: Thromboelastography following Concizumab (HzTFPI4F36) Exposure in Hemophilia A-like (anti-FVIII antibody treated) Human Blood Derived from Healthy Male Donors
Concentrations: 0.01nM to 50nM
Control: Buffer and huFVIIa (10nM)	Concizumab elicited hemostatic activity with a half-maximal effect in the low nM range (0.4nM and 1nM for reduced clotting time and improved clot development, respectively) in human blood derived from healthy male donors that was treated (ex vivo) with a neutralizing anti-FVIII antibody to mimic the hemophilia A condition.
The thromboelastography results indicate that concizumab has a pro-hemostatic effect on (male) human blood (in vitro) and may elicit hemostatic effects in patients with hemophilia A.
L1198: Study Details	Findings
Study No.: BBSN100201
Study Title: Thromboelastography following Concizumab (NNC 0172-2021) exposure in Hemophilia A-like (anti-FVIII antibody treated) and Hemophilia B-like (anti-FIX antibody treated) Human Blood Derived from Healthy Male Donors.
Concentrations: 0.01nM to 50nM
Controls: Normal Blood and Buffer	Concizumab elicited a comparable level of hemostatic activity with regards to reduced clotting times (0.4nM, EC50) and improved clot development (2.2nM to 2.6nM, EC50) in human blood derived from healthy male donors that was treated (ex vivo) with either a neutralizing anti-FVIII antibody or a neutralizing anti-FIX antibody to mimic the hemophilia A and hemophilia B conditions, respectively.
The thromboelastography results indicate that concizumab has a pro-hemostatic effect in (male) human blood (in vitro) and suggests that concizumab may elicit similar hemostatic effects in patients with hemophilia A or hemophilia B.
Study No.: BBSN091002
Study Title: Thromboelastography Analysis of Concizumab (NNC 0172-2021) in Hemophilia A-like (anti-FVIII antibody treated) Blood Derived from Healthy Male Cynomolgus Monkeys.
Concentrations: 0.01nM to 50nM
Controls: Normal Blood and Buffer	Concizumab elicited a comparable level (versus humans) of hemostatic activity with regards to reduced clotting times (0.4nM, EC50) and improved clot development (0.5nM, EC50) in blood derived from healthy male cynomolgus monkeys that was treated (ex vivo) with a neutralizing anti-FVIII antibody to mimic the hemophilia A condition.
The thromboelastography results indicate that concizumab has a pro-hemostatic effect in (male) cynomolgus monkey blood (in vitro) and supports the premise that the cynomolgus monkey is a pharmacologically relevant animal species.
Study No.: 209144
Study Title: Neutralization of TFPI Activity in a Fxa Activity Assay (In Vitro) by Concizumab (HzTFPI4F36).
Concentrations: 0.24nM to 15nM
Controls: Assay buffer that included all FX activation assay components (minimum activity) or assay buffer with all components but excluding TFPI (maximum activity).	Concizumab neutralized (EC50, 0.8nM) a fixed concentration of human TFPI (6nM) that in the absence of concizumab would be expected to achieve a 60% to 90% reduction in Fxa activity.
Concizumab increased human Fxa activity in a concentration dependent manner and at concentrations exceeding 1.8nM the Fxa activity was increased above the maximum anticipated level of Fxa activity (> 100%). No explanation for the observed super-activity was provided however the result is consistent with the findings that neutralization of TFPI may result in increased and/or more sustained activity of FVIIa/Fxa (in vitro).
The mean plasma TFPI concentration in healthy humans ranges from 1.6nM to 2.5nM and suggests that concizumab may completely neutralize the TFPI activity (facilitating unrestricted Fxa activity) in human plasma at circulating antibody concentrations in the low nM range.
L1199: Study Details	Findings
Study No.: 210180
Study Title: Neutralization of TFPI
Activity in a FVIIa/TF/Fxa Activity Assay (In Vitro) by Concizumab (NNC 0172-2021).
Concentrations: 0.16nM to 30nM
Controls: Assay buffer that included all FX activation assay components (minimum activity) or assay buffer with all components but excluding TFPI (maximum activity).	Concizumab neutralized (EC_{50}, 2.8nM) a fixed concentration of human TFPI (1nM) that in the absence of concizumab elicited a 70% to 90% reduction in the FVIIa/TF/Fxa complex activity.
Concizumab increased human FVIIa/TF/Fxa complex activity in a concentration dependent manner and as concizumab was not preincubated with TFPI this assay reflects the direct competition between Fxa and concizumab for the TFPI target substrate.
The mean plasma TFPI concentration in healthy humans ranges from 1.6nM to 2.5nM and suggests that concizumab may completely neutralize the TFPI activity (facilitating unrestricted FVIIa/TF/Fxa complex activity) in human plasma at circulating antibody concentrations in the low nM range.
Study No.: LCP080802
Study Title: Functional In Vitro Characterization of Concizumab (NNC 0172-0000-2021-1A) Neutralization of Endothelial Cell Associated TFPI Activity (Inhibition of TF/FVIIa-mediated activation of FX) Measured in a FX Activation Assay.
Cell Lines: Human Cell Line and Primary Cells: ECV304 cells and TNF\alpha/IL1\beta-stimulated HUVECs, respectively.
Concentrations: 0.04nM to 5nM
Concentrations: 0.3nM to 20nM
Control: Assay buffer that included all FX activation assay components (minimum activity).	Concizumab neutralized cell associated TFPI activity (the inhibition of TF/FVIIa-mediated activation of FX) in a concentration dependent manner (EC_{50}, less than 0.5nM) in TNF\alpha / IL1\beta stimulated (to induce TF expression) HUVEC cells and in ECV304 cells (constitutively express low levels of TF).
The murine precursor of concizumab (MuTFPI4F36) elicited a comparable result in ECV304 cells.
These data indicate that concizumab is capable of neutralizing the activity (inhibition of TF/FVIIa-mediated FX activation) of the cell-associated (glycosyl)phosphatidylinositol (GPI) anchored form of TFPI that specifically inhibits TF-mediated activity when TF is expressed on the same cell as the TFPI protein.
Study No.: MTBH100403
Study Title: Residual TFPI Functionality Measured in Human Plasma Spiked with Concizumab
Species: NZW Rabbit, Cynomolgus Monkey and Human
Concentrations: 0.63nM to 630nM
Control: Actichrome TFPI activity assay buffer and components (minimum activity).	Concizumab neutralized TFPI activity (the inhibition of the catalytic activity of Fxa) in a concentration dependent manner (EC_{50}, 6nM) in pooled human plasma (10-fold diluted samples).
Note: As dilution linearity was not evaluated, the EC_{50} could vary if other dilution factors are employed.
The EC_{50} values for concizumab inhibition of TFPI activity were estimated using FVIII-inhibited rabbit plasma (5.8nM) and monkey plasma (1.6nM) and were comparable to the human EC_{50} value. The optimal dilution factors with respect to the best Fxa response were found to be a 10-fold dilution for rabbit samples and a 5-fold dilution for monkey samples in human TFPI depleted plasma. Dilution linearity was not observed with either rabbit plasma or monkey plasma and as the EC_{50}s varied widely when applying different dilution factors in these species similar variability would be expected with human samples.
L1200: Study Details	Findings
Study No.: LCP080102
Study Title: In Vitro Characterization of the Mouse Monoclonal Antibody 4F36 including the Neutralization of TFPI in the presence of various concentrations of rFVIII in Tissue Factor-Induced Thrombin Generation and Fibrin Clot Formation in FVIII-depleted Plasma supplemented with phospholipids or platelets Measured by Endogenous Thrombin Potential (ETP), Thromboelastography (TEG) and Calibrated Automated Thrombin (CAT) Generation.
Species: Human
Concentration: 10nM (4F36)
Concentrations: 0.005-1 IU/mL (rFVIII)
Control: Assay buffer that included all of the CAT generation components and increasing concentrations (0 to 1.5 U/mL) of FVIII replenishment.
Control: Assay buffer that included TEG components and increasing concentrations (0 to 1 U/mL) of recombinant FVIII.	The murine precursor of concizumab (MuTFPI4F36) promoted coagulation (thrombin generation and TEG) in the presence of low concentrations of FVIII (consistent with the conditions present in severe and moderate hemophilia A plasma). The extent of coagulation was comparable to that achieved when normal levels of FVIII were restored (1 IU/mL) and the effect of MuTFPI4F36 was more pronounced at subtherapeutic levels of FVIII with only a minimal effect at the highest concentration.
The thrombin generation pattern of 4F36 was distinctly different from the pattern obtained by replenishment of FVIII-depleted plasma with rFVIII and indicated that the thrombin formed in patients with hemophilia under the influence of concizumab may be more susceptible to fibrinolysis than thrombin formed under normal conditions or with FVIII supplementation.
These results suggest that concizumab can promote thrombin burst formation in the presence of low concentrations of FVIII (in vitro). Concizumab is also being indicated for hemophilia B (Factor IX deficiency) where patients maintain normal levels of functional FVIII. Normal levels of FVIII may result in diminished coagulation and are consistent with the results observed with pooled plasma from patients with HB that showed a negative DDI effect when concizumab was combined with rFIX (321538).
Study No.: 215093
Study Title: Evaluation of TFPI Binding and Release from Endothelial Cells using Concizumab via Flow Cytometry and ELISA.
Cell Lines: Human ECV304 and EA.hy926 endothelial cells
Concentrations: 10, 100, or 1000nM
Controls: Assay buffer, Heparin (5 U/mL, positive control), TFPI Kunitz domain 3 specific mAb (positive control mAb) and a trinitrophenol specific mAb (negative control)	Concizumab (up to 1000nM) bound to TFPI on the cell surface of endothelial cells (EC_{50} of 0.1nM) and elicited a concentration dependent release of TFPI that was significantly lower than the level of release achieved with heparin (5 U/mL) or a mAb directed against the Kunitz domain 3 (C-terminus) of TFPI.
The predominant form of TFPI on the endothelial cell surface is TFPI\beta, that lacks a portion in the C-terminal region and instead is comprised of an alternative carboxy terminus with a glycosyl phosphatidyl inositol (GPI) anchor.
These results are consistent with the mechanism required for the release of cell surface bound TFPI that involves association with the C-terminal region (not the K2 domain) of the TFPI\alpha form (that exhibits reduced surface expression) of the protein and suggests that the extent of concizumab-mediated release of TFPI from the endothelial cell surface (an anticoagulant effect) will be minimal.
L1201: Study Details	Findings
Study No.: BLZ100301
Study Title: Effect of Concizumab (HzTFPI4F36) on Cuticle Bleeding in Pseudo/Transient (Anti FVIII-Treated) Hemophilia A Female NZW Rabbits
Species: NZW Rabbit (Female)
Doses: 1 to 8 mg/kg (IV)
Control: Buffer with isotype control (IgG) antibody (4 mg/kg)	Concizumab (administered IV) 35 minutes before induction of cuticle bleeding) dose-dependently reduced bleeding time (statistically significant only at the HD) and reduced blood loss (statistically significant at doses greater than or equal to 4 mg/kg) in transient hemophilic female NZW rabbits (administered a neutralizing anti-FVIII antibody 45 minutes prior to bleed induction) over a 1-hr observation period. No signs of adverse effects (e.g., decrease in platelet numbers) were present.
The maximum efficacious dose with regards to reduced bleeding time in pseudo-HA female NZW rabbits was 8 mg/kg (HD).
Study No.: BLZ110403/ BLZ110701
Study Title: Dose (IV) Response of Concizumab (mAb 2021) on Cuticle Bleeding in Pseudo/Transient (Anti FVIII-Treated) Hemophilia A Female NZW Rabbits
Species: NZW Rabbit (Female)
Doses: 0.125 to 1 mg/kg (IV)
Control: Buffer with isotype control (IgG) antibody (1 mg/kg)	Blood loss was reduced dose-dependently when concizumab was administered (IV) 35 minutes prior to induction of cuticle bleeding in transient hemophilic female NZW rabbits (administered a neutralizing anti-FVIII antibody 45 minutes prior to bleed induction) over a 1-hour observation period. The maximum efficacious dose (MED) was reached at 0.375 mg/kg (statistical significance was not achieved at any dose level) and the ED50 was 0.23 mg/kg.
The maximum effect achieved in this study was not significantly different from the maximum effect achieved at higher doses (up to 8 mg/kg) in prior studies and indicates that doses exceeding 0.375 mg/kg are not required to achieve maximal efficacy in the NZW rabbit cuticle bleed model.
Study No.: BLZ090801
Study Title: Effect of "On-Demand" Treatment (IV) with Concizumab (HzTFPI4F36) on Cuticle Bleeding (Ongoing Bleeding Model) in Pseudo/Transient (Anti FVIII-Treated) Hemophilia A Female NZW Rabbits
Species: NZW Rabbit (Female)
Doses: 2 mg/kg (IV)
Control: Buffer with isotype control (IgG) antibody (2 mg/kg) or rFVIIa (9 mg/kg)	"On-Demand" treatment (initiated 5 minutes after bleed induction) with concizumab (2 mg/kg, IV) significantly reduced blood loss from an ongoing cuticle bleeding event in transient hemophilic female NZW rabbits (administered a neutralizing anti-FVIII antibody 45 minutes prior to bleed induction). The extent of reduced blood loss and bleeding time (over the 1-hour observation period) were comparable between concizumab and rFVIIa (9 mg/kg, IV) and the reduction in bleeding time was not statistically significant for either compound.
The results indicate that the procoagulant effects of concizumab can be achieved when administered shortly after injury induction (within 5 minutes) in the NZW rabbit cuticle bleed model.
L1202: Study Details	Findings
Study No.: BLZ100602
Study Title: Effect of "On-Demand" Treatment (IV) with Concizumab (NNC 0172-0000-2021) on Cuticle Bleeding (Ongoing Bleeding Model) in Pseudo/Transient (Anti FVIII-Treated) Hemophilia A Female NZW Rabbits
Species: NZW Rabbit (Female)
Doses: 0.5 to 4 mg/kg (IV)
Control: Buffer with isotype control (IgG) antibody (4 mg/kg)	"On-Demand" treatment (initiated 5 minutes after bleed induction) with concizumab significantly reduced blood loss from an ongoing cuticle bleeding event in transient hemophilic female NZW rabbits (administered a neutralizing anti-FVIII antibody 45 minutes prior to bleed induction) at doses greater than or equal to 1 mg/kg (IV) over a 1-hour observation period. The effect at the LD (0.5 mg/kg) was not statistical significance and a significant reduction in bleeding time was present only at the MD (2 mg/kg).
The results indicate that the procoagulant effects of concizumab can be achieved at doses of 1 mg/kg or higher when administered (IV) shortly after injury induction (within 5 minutes) in the NZW rabbit cuticle bleed model.
Study No.: 209164 (BLZ090101)
Study Title: Effect of (IV) Concizumab (HzTFPI4F36) on Cuticle Bleeding in Pseudo/Transient (Anti FVIII-Treated) Hemophilia A Female NZW Rabbits (PK/PD Study)
Note: Duration of Action Study
Species: NZW Rabbit (Female)
Doses: 20 mg/kg (IV)
Control: Buffer with isotype control (IgG) antibody (20 mg/kg, reduced to 2 mg/kg in some control rabbits due to a lack of antibody availability)	A single (IV) dose (20 mg/kg) of concizumab (followed by treatment (on Day 4, 7 or 10) with a neutralizing anti-FVIII mAb to induce pseudo hemophilia before the induction of cuticle bleeding 45 minutes later) reduced bleeding (blood loss and bleeding time) in transient hemophilic female NZW rabbits over a 1-hour observation period on Day 4 and Day 7. No hemostatic effects were observed on Day 10 when concizumab levels were found to be significantly reduced in the plasma. No signs of adverse effects (e.g., decrease in platelet numbers) were observed.
The results indicate that the procoagulant effects of concizumab are durable for up to 7 days after a single (IV) dose of 20 mg/kg (administered prior to injury induction) in the NZW rabbit cuticle bleed model. The observed pharmacokinetic profile indicated that concizumab was cleared by target mediated mechanisms after 10 days and the decline in plasma concentrations corresponded to a loss in pharmacodynamic activity.
Study No.: BLZ090501
Study Title: Effect of Treatment (SC) with Concizumab on Cuticle Bleeding in Pseudo/Transient (Anti FVIII-Treated) Hemophilia A Female NZW Rabbits.
Species: NZW Rabbit (Female)
Doses: 10 mg/kg (SC)
Control: Buffer with isotype control (IgG) antibody (10 mg/kg)	A single subcutaneous (SC) dose (10 mg/kg) of concizumab reduced bleeding time and blood loss significantly (over a 1-hour observation period) when administered 24 hrs prior to induction of bleeding in transient hemophilic female NZW rabbits. No signs of adverse effects (e.g., decrease in platelet numbers) were observed.
The results indicate that the procoagulant effects of concizumab can be achieved after a single (SC) dose (the clinical ROA) of 10 mg/kg when administered 24 hrs prior to injury induction in the NZW rabbit cuticle bleed model. The observed pharmacokinetic profile indicated that plasma concizumab levels (40 µg/mL) were relatively stable in rabbits over the 1-hour observation period and corresponded to the observed pharmacodynamic activity.
L1203: Study Details	Findings
Study No.: PJOH090201
Study Title: Acute Effects of (IV) Concizumab (NNC 0172-0000-2021) a humanized anti-TFPI antibody on Clot Formation and Coagulation Markers in a Venous Stasis Model in Normal (non-hemophilic) Female NZW Rabbits
Species: NZW Rabbit (Female)
Doses: 2 mg/kg (IV)
Control: Buffer with isotype control (IgG) antibody (2 mg/kg)	The frequency of distal clot formation and the size of clots present locally in the ligated facial veins of normal female NZW rabbits were comparable between the animals administered concizumab or an isotype control antibody (10 minute prior to clamp ligation).
There were no statistically significant differences in platelet counts, PT, TAT, and fibrinogen levels. A minimal decrease in APTT and fibrinogen degradation products (relative to control) was observed with concizumab.
Lungs thrombi (noted microscopically) were present only in concizumab-dosed rabbits (50%) and were considered potentially related to the administration of concizumab.
Study No.: PJOH090802
Study Title: Acute Effects of (IV) Concizumab (NNC 0172-0000-2021) a humanized anti-TFPI antibody on Local Clot Formation in a Venous Stasis Model in Female NZW Rabbits Following Vessel Wall Injury and Prevention with a Low Molecular Weight Heparin (LMWH).
Species: NZW Rabbit (Female)
Dose: 2 mg/kg (IV) Concizumab
Dose: 500 IU/kg (IV) LMWH
Control: Saline buffer with isotype control (IgG) antibody (2 mg/kg)	Prophylactic treatment with concizumab significantly increased local clot formation in healthy female NZW rabbits when facial veins were crushed and then ligated 30 minutes later.
When concizumab dosing was followed by (5 minutes later) the administration of LMWH (before injury) local clot formation was prevented.
No changes in coagulation parameters were observed (platelet counts, WBC counts, PT, and fibrinogen). A minimal decrease in APTT was observed after concizumab (only) dosing.
These results indicate that LMWH can prevent the procoagulant effects of concizumab in non-hemophilic rabbits and suggest that LMWH may be used as a preventive treatment option if complications caused by exaggerated pharmacology arise.
L1204: Study Details	Findings
Secondary Pharmacodynamics	
Study No.: HHBP100601
Study Title: In Vitro Effects of Concizumab on Protein C
Activation and Antithrombin Activity using Human Umbilical Vein Endothelial Cells (HUVECs)
Cell Line: HUVECs
Concentrations: 350nM (50µg/mL) to 1.75µM (250µg/mL).
Controls: Assay buffer with all components (positive control). Anti-Thrombomodulin Antibody, No Thrombin, No Protein C, and No HUVECs (negative controls).	Concizumab bound to (0.42nM or 0.06µg/mL, K_D) surface expressed TFPI on HUVEC cells (ELISA) and indicated that surface associated TFPI would be saturated at the concentrations employed in the subsequent coagulation activity assessments (\ge 350nM or 5 µg/mL).
After incubating (30 min) HUVECs with thrombin and protein C (with or without antithrombin), concizumab did not affect thrombin mediated protein C activation or antithrombin inhibition of thrombin. However, effects on the initial rate (0 to 30 min) of antithrombin-mediated thrombin inhibition were not assessed.
These results indicate that two independent negative regulators (activated protein C and antithrombin) of the coagulation cascade (which function downstream and independent of TFPI) remained functional in the presence of concizumab and its saturation of cell associated TFPI (in vitro).

L1207: ## 13.1.1.2. Safety Pharmacology
L1208: Safety pharmacology studies were conducted as part of the pivotal 13-week toxicity study with an 11-week recovery in cynomolgus monkeys (See Study 209206 for a summarized report of findings in Section 13.1.2.1). In brief, neurobehavioral observations which included extended clinical signs (CNS endpoints), respiratory evaluations (visual appraisal of respiratory rate/depth) and a cytokine release assessment (IL-2, IL-4, IL-5, IL-6, TNF-\alpha, IFN-\gamma) were all unremarkable.
L1209: Cardiovascular assessments (blood pressure, heart rates, electrocardiogram (ECG) intervals, and the morphology of the ECG waveform) and urinalysis were conducted during the pivotal 13-week and 52-week repeat-dose toxicity studies in cynomolgus monkeys, and no effects of concizumab were observed up to the highest doses assessed (200 mg/kg/QW, intravenous (IV) and 50 mg/kg/day, SC).
L1210: Safety pharmacology assessments conducted in vitro consisted of human Fc receptor binding, antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC), complement fixation and binding to peripheral blood cells and are described in detail below.
L1211: **Table 58. Safety Pharmacology Studies (In Vitro)**
L1212: Study Details	Findings
Study No: 210178
Study Title: In Vitro Binding to Human Fc Gamma Receptors by Surface Plasmon Resonance (SPR)
GLP Compliance Status: Non-GLP
Species: Human
Concentrations: 3.9 to 1000nM (FcR)
Control: Assay buffer with isotype control (IgG4) mAb	FcγRI (CD64) bound to immobilized (monovalent) concizumab with a KD of 2nM. The dissociation constants (KDs) for the other Fc gamma receptors assessed: FcRn (H-274), FcγRIIa (131R and 131H variants, CD32a), FcγRIIb (CD32b), and FcγRIIIa (158V variant, CD16b, genetic polymorphism) ranged from 0.4µM to 1.7µM and negligible binding to FcγRIIIa (158F variant, CD16b, genetic polymorphism) was achieved.
The FcR gamma binding constants (KD) for immobilized (monovalent) concizumab were comparable to those achieved with a human IgG4 control antibody and suggest that the specific attributes of the concizumab mAb do not alter normal FcR gamma interactions under the conditions of this assay.
Study No.: 209148
Study Title: Antibody-Dependent Cell-Mediated Cytotoxicity (ADCC) by concizumab (NNC 0172-0000-2021)
GLP Compliance Status: Non-GLP
Species: Human Endothelial Cell Line (EA.hy926WT) Primary HUVECs
Concentrations: 0.64 ng/mL to 250µg/mL
Control: Assay buffer with isotype control (IgG4) mAb or an anti-TFPI IgG1 control mAb (positive control for ADCC)	Concizumab bound to TFPI expressed on the surface of an endothelial cell line (EA.hy926WT) and on primary HUVECs (independent of the state of TNF\square stimulation) to a degree that was similar to an anti-TFPI IgG1 control mAb by flow cytometry.
Concizumab did not facilitate cell lysis by peripheral blood mononuclear effector cells (PBMCs) in either case and the IgG1 control antibody functioned as expected (induced cytotoxicity via PBMC effector cells).
These results indicate that concizumab does not promote ADCC (via lysis of antibody-coated human endothelial cells) initiated by Fc-receptor interactions with effector cells (PBMCs) under these in vitro conditions.
Study No.: 209134
Study Title: Evaluation of the Ability of Anti-TFPI Monoclonal Antibodies to Activate the Complement Cascade (In Vitro) in a Complement Fixation ELISA Assay
GLP Compliance Status: Non-GLP
Species: Human
Concentrations: 0.5 to 500 ng/mL
Controls: Assay buffer with all components (negative control). Anti-TFPI serum from rabbit 2460A (positive control).	Concizumab (in complex with full length human TFPI) did not bind to complement component 4 (C4) or facilitate the activation of the complement cascade under the conditions of the complement fixation ELISA assay (in vitro).
Binding of C4 was detected with the positive control rabbit polyclonal antibody (2460A) and was likely driven by the presence of IgG1 isotypes that preferentially bind to complement component 1q (C1q).
These results are consistent with the IgG4 isotype of concizumab which (in general) does not bind to C1q and is therefore not expected to initiate activation of the complement system and the subsequent recruitment and cleavage of C4.
L1213: Study Details	Findings
Study No.: 209137	Flow cytometric analysis revealed that concizumab does not significantly bind to human peripheral blood cell subsets (granulocytes, monocytes, lymphocytes, thrombocytes, or erythrocytes) that do not express significant levels of TFPI on their cell surface.
Study Title: Flow Cytometric Evaluation of Binding of Concizumab to Human Peripheral Blood Cells and Human Endothelial Cell Lines	
GLP Compliance Status: Non-GLP	These findings are consistent with the results that showed that concizumab selectively binds to the TFPI expressing endothelial cell line (EAHy926WT) but not to the TFPI negative cell line (EAHy926AR).
Species: Human

L1216: ## 13.1.1.3. Pharmacodynamic Drug Interactions
L1217: **Table 59. Pharmacodynamic Drug Interaction Studies**
L1218: Study Details	Findings
Study No.: 300077	Co-administration (IV) of concizumab and rFVIIa in a cuticle bleeding model in NZW rabbits (administered a neutralizing anti-FVIII antibody 25 minutes prior to bleed induction to transiently induce Hemophilia A) did not result in any clear additive or synergistic effects across 4 different sub-studies.
Sub Studies: BLz120302, BLz120304, BLz120601 and BLz120901.	
Study Title: Effect Studies with the Combination of Concizumab and rFVIIa in a Cuticle Bleeding Model in Rabbits with Transient (Anti FVIII- Antibody-Induced) Hemophilia A.	
Note: Extended Bleed Induction	BLz120302 (dosed 2 mg/kg concizumab + 9 mg/kg rFVIIa, 15 min after bleed induction)
Species: NZW Rabbit (Female)	BLz120304 (dosed 1 mg/kg concizumab + 9 mg/kg rFVIIa, 5 min prior to bleed induction)
Doses: Concizumab: 0.25 to 2 mg/kg	BLz120601 (dosed 0.25 mg/kg concizumab + 1 mg/kg rFVIIa, 5 min prior to bleed induction)
Doses: rFVIIa: 1 to 9 mg/kg	BLz120901 (dosed 2 mg/kg concizumab + 1 to 9 mg/kg rFVIIa, 30 min after bleed induction)
Controls: Assay buffer with isotype control (IgG) antibody (2 mg/kg)	Procoagulant effects were not observed when concizumab was administered after an extended bleed induction (15-30 min). In addition, there were no signs of adverse effects (e.g., decreases in platelet counts (up to 1-hour post-dose) and suggests that the degree of coagulation activation and consumption (e.g., associated with disseminated intravascular coagulation, DIC) was minimal in rabbits.
Due to the nature and design of these rabbit studies, it is not possible to fully exclude an additive or synergistic effect of rFVIIa and concizumab and precautions should be taken when patients receiving concizumab prophylaxis experience break-through bleeding events and require treatment with rFVIIa (or another bypassing agent).

L1220: Note: The clinical pharmacodynamic drug interaction (in vitro/ex vivo) studies (319076 and 321538) have been reviewed separately by the clinical pharmacology discipline.

L1222: ## 28-Day (Pharmacokinetic/Pharmacodynamic) Drug-Drug Interaction (Concizumab and rFVIIa) Toxicity Study in Cynomolgus Monkeys (Study 215431, GLP)
L1223: ## Key Study Findings:
L1224: - Administration of rFVIIa (IV) to cynomolgus monkeys at doses \le 1 mg/kg (three times with two-hour intervals) on Day 28 in the presence or absence of steady state concentrations of concizumab was well tolerated, with no dose-related pathological findings (e.g., no thrombi)
- Concizumab and rFVIIa were pharmacologically active in cynomolgus monkeys as indicated by the observed reduction in plasma fibrinogen, free TFPI, and residual TFPI functionality and the shortening of prothrombin times (PT), respectively.
- Thrombin/anti-thrombin (TAT) complex and D-dimer concentrations in the plasma tended to increase in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab or rFVIIa alone and the effects of
L1225: rFVIIa in the presence of steady state concentrations of concizumab were shown to be additive with respect to these markers of coagulation
L1226: *Reviewer Comments: The pharmacological effects of concizumab (SC) and rFVIIa (IV) tended to be additive (when combined) with respect to clotting parameters and steady state concentrations of concizumab and did not adversely impact the safety profile of rFVIIa in cynomolgus monkeys.*

L1231: **Table 61. Observations and Results, Study 215431, GLP**
L1232: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	There were no unscheduled deaths in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at 1 mg/kg/day (SC) followed by 3 doses of rFVIIa at 2-hour intervals of 0.25, 0.5 or 1 mg/kg (Day 28 Only, IV Bolus).
Clinical signs	Transient reddening of the SC injection site on the upper back of a single male (#10) was noted on Day 8 only (non-adverse) and clinical signs were otherwise unremarkable.
Body weights and food consumption	Unremarkable
Hematology	To evaluate the effect of rFVIIa in the presence of a steady state concentration of concizumab compared to rFVIIa alone, data from Day 26 was compared with data from after rFVIIa dosing (Day 28 2 hours post first rFVIIa dose; Day 28 two hours post third rFVIIa dose; Day 29 - necropsy).
L1233: Parameters	Major Findings
(RBC Parameter): Erythroid parameters (hemoglobin, red blood cells, packed cell volumes and mean cell volumes) tended to decline with repeat blood sampling (Day 28 and Day 29) and mean values on Day 26 were similar to pretreatment. The decline in RBC parameters was considered driven by the frequent blood sampling (not test article related).
(WBC Parameters): A similar decline was observed in platelet counts in all dosing groups (including controls).

Coagulation	
Prothrombin Time (PT) and Activated Partial Thromboplastin Time (APTT): There were no notable changes in PT and APTT in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (only) for 4 weeks at 1 mg/kg/day (SC). A shortening of PT was observed in both sexes at 2 hours after the third dose of rFVIIa (only) and rebounded thereafter. Prothrombin Time (independent of dose level of rFVIIa) was shortened in both sexes in the presence of concizumab at 2 hours after the first rFVIIa administration and persisted after the third administration at the HD (1 mg/kg) only. Prothrombin time reductions at the HD (1 mg/kg, rFVIIa) in the presence of steady state concizumab were comparable to those seen with rFVIIa only and normalized on Day 29 (slightly exceeding baseline values).
Fibrinogen: A reduction in fibrinogen was present in monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at 1 mg/kg/day (SC).
(Thrombin Antithrombin (TAT) Complex): Thrombin antithrombin complex is a protein complex of thrombin and antithrombin and is a marker of net activation of coagulation. Thrombin antithrombin complex (TAT) concentrations (in general) increased in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (only) for 4 weeks at 1 mg/kg/day (SC). On Day 28, after treatment with rFVIIa (2-hrs after first and third administrations) in the presence of concizumab the average TAT concentration was increased further (dose-dependently) and indicated an additive effect.

L1240: Thrombin Antithrombin (TAT) and D-dimer concentrations were notably reduced in a single male (#8) that was shown to be ADA positive, exhibited reduced concizumab exposures and elevated TFPI activity. After administration of rFVIIa, this male was capable of generating increases in TAT and D-dimers suggesting that the ADAs to concizumab did not interfere with the activity of rFVIIa.
L1241: (Anti-Thrombin (AT) Concentrations): AT concentrations were generally unaffected by treatment with concizumab and declined minimally after the 1st/3rd doses of rFVIIa on Day 28 and through Day 29 (independent of dose). There were no notable effects on AT after dosing with rFVIIa in the absence of concizumab.

L1247: (Free Tissue Factor Pathway Inhibitor [TFPI] Concentrations): Detectable levels of free TFPI were present in all cynomolgus monkeys at pre-treatment and in controls on Days 26 and 29, with no apparent influence of dosing with rFVIIa (Day 29). On Days 26 and 29, free TFPI values were below the lower limit of detection for all concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys barring the ADA positive male (#8) where quantifiable levels were present.
L1248: (Residual TFPI Functionality [Chromogenic] assay): Residual TFPI functionality was measured in all cynomolgus monkeys during pre-treatment, and the residual TFPI functionality decreased in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab from Day 2 forward (often below the lower limit of quantification), particularly in males. The residual TFPI functionality was not altered significantly by rFVIIa treatment.
L1249: Parameters	Major Findings
(Protein C and Protein S): Plasma protein C/S concentrations were unaffected by treatment with concizumab and/or rFVIIa.
Clinical chemistry	There were no notable changes in clinical chemistry parameters in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at 1 mg/kg/day (SC) followed by 3 doses of rFVIIa at 2-hour intervals of 0.25, 0.5 or 1 mg/kg (Day 28 Only, IV Bolus).
Anti-drug antibody (ADA) analysis	Low levels of ADAs (close to the assay cut point), were detected at pre-treatment (#109 and #9) without explanation and on Day 29 (#9). Anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) detected in male #8 on Day 29, were deemed related to concizumab dosing and the Optical Density (OD) value in the ADA assay in male #8 exceeded the assay cut point by 10-fold. No ADAs were detected in the other cynomolgus monkeys in this study however the negative results may be due to interference from high concentrations of concizumab in plasma samples (leading to false negative results).
Toxicokinetics	(Concizumab)
The plasma exposure to concizumab tended to be lower in females than in males and the median t_{max} was 24 hours (Day 1) and ranged from 4 hours to 6 hours on Day 27. Concizumab exposures were reduced in one male (#8) that developed ADAs.
(rFVIIa)
The mean terminal half-life ranged from 1.4 hours to 1.8 hours and there was no apparent difference in exposure to rFVIIa between the sexes. The FVIIa clot activity increased with ascending dose of rFVIIa (dose-proportional). There was no apparent difference in the toxicokinetics of 1 mg/kg rFVIIa administered in the presence or absence of steady state concizumab.
Gross pathology	Subcutaneous and intravenous injection sites (saphenous vein) and sampling sites (foreleg and inguinal areas) were found to be red and corresponded with the microscopic finding of hemorrhage at these sites. These findings were considered consistent with the injection procedures and venipuncture.
Organ weights	Organ weight changes in the testis, epididymis, prostate, seminal vesicles, ovary, and uterus were deemed to reflect the different stages of sexual maturity in the adolescent cynomolgus monkeys used on study rather than a direct effect of concizumab.
L1250: Parameters	Major Findings
Histopathology	(Subcutaneous Injection Site): Inflammatory cells (lymphocytic, plasmacytic cell infiltration) at the concizumab (SC) injection site were increased (dose independent) in both sexes and the infiltration was minimal at the HD (1 mg/kg) of rFVIIa when administered in the presence of steady state concizumab. These findings were present to some extent in rFVIIa only and vehicle controls and were therefore considered related to the injection procedure rather than a direct effect of either test-article.
Adequate battery: yes	(Thrombi): There were no thrombi observed in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at 1 mg/kg/day (SC) followed by 3 doses of rFVIIa at 2-hr intervals of 0.25, 0.5 or 1 mg/kg (Day 28 Only, IV Bolus).
L1251: Source: Reviewer constructed summary table of the toxicology Study (215431) submitted to the BLA.
Abbreviations: ADA, anti-drug antibody; APTT, activated partial thromboplastin time; AT, anti-thrombin; IV, intravenous; MDDM, D-Dimer; OD, optical density; PT, prothrombin time; RBC, red blood cell; rFVIIa, recombinant factor VIIa; SC, subcutaneous; TAT, thrombin antithrombin; TFPI, tissue factor pathway inhibitor; tmax, time to reach maximum concentration; WBC, white blood cell
L1252: ## 13.1.1.4. ADME/Pharmacokinetics
L1253: Overall, the nonclinical data characterizing the absorption, distribution, metabolism, and elimination of concizumab supported to use of the selected nonclinical species (New Zealand White (NZW) rabbits and cynomolgus monkeys), allowed for interspecies exposure comparisons and provided relevant information to determine clinical dose selection.
L1254: ## Absorption
L1255: The pharmacokinetics of concizumab was evaluated in NZW rabbits and cynomolgus monkeys after single intravenous (IV) and subcutaneous (SC) administration. Repeat-dose pharmacokinetics and toxicokinetics were assessed in cynomolgus monkeys.
L1256: **Table 65. Pharmacokinetic Parameters of Concizumab (Single Dose (SD) Intravenous (IV) Administration)**

Abbreviations: AUCinf, area under the concentration-time curve from time zero to infinity; NR, not reported; NZW, New Zealand White; t1/2, terminal half-life
L1259: The clearance and volume of distribution of concizumab decreased with increasing (IV) dose in NZW rabbits and cynomolgus monkeys indicating non-linear pharmacokinetics. There were no
L1260: notable differences in the single dose pharmacokinetics between the sexes in cynomolgus monkeys (210156).
L1261: Lower doses of concizumab were cleared more rapidly from the circulation in cynomolgus monkeys, while at higher doses the plasma concentration profile became flat until a concentration of 10-20\mug/mL, at which the elimination rate increased. This pattern is consistent with target-mediated drug disposition (TMDD) where a compound is cleared from the circulation when binding to its target (typically a cell-bound target) in this case most likely the TFPI bound to the surface of endothelial cells. At higher concentrations, the TMDD of concizumab is saturated, resulting in a slower clearance and a flat plasma concentration profile with a half-life of 200 hours in the cynomolgus monkey, which is consistent with the pharmacokinetic characteristics of monoclonal antibodies. At lower concentrations, the target-mediated clearance of concizumab accelerates and plasma concentrations decrease rapidly.
L1262: Exposure increased with ascending dose level in NZW rabbits and cynomolgus monkeys when concizumab was administered as a single subcutaneous (SC) dose. The t_{max} increased with ascending dose from 36 hours (2 mg/kg) to 120 hours (20 mg/kg) in NZW rabbits and supports the premise of TMDD. Bioavailability was found to be 80% in NZW rabbits and cynomolgus monkeys.

L1267: ## Repeat-Dose Toxicokinetics
L1268: The toxicokinetics of concizumab was assessed as a part of a Dose Range Finding (DRF) study (SC) and the five repeat-dose toxicity studies (SC/IV) of 4, 13, 26, or 52 weeks in duration conducted in cynomolgus monkeys at dose levels ranging from 0.5 mg/kg to 200 mg/kg. Daily administration (SC) of concizumab was employed in repeat-dose toxicology studies in an attempt to overcome the effects of TMDD (particularly at low doses) and maintain target (TFPI) saturation.

L1275: ## Distribution
L1276: No dedicated distribution studies were conducted with concizumab. The distribution of concizumab was evaluated by immunohistochemistry in rabbits (Hansen et al. 2014), tissue cross-reactivity studies (human and monkey), and immunohistochemistry in cynomolgus monkeys. In general, concizumab was found to be present in the blood and associated with the vascular endothelium, with only minor differences between humans and cynomolgus monkeys.
L1277: ## Pilot Tissue Cross-Reactivity (TCR) Study of Concizumab with Limited Normal Human and Cynomolgus Monkey Tissues (Study 209125, Non-GLP)
L1278: ## Key Study Findings
L1279: - The concizumab staining pattern was (in general) similar between the human and cynomolgus monkey tissues examined and corresponded to known sites of TFPI tissue expression.
- The noted differences observed between humans and cynomolgus monkeys with regards to concizumab tissue staining patterns (e.g., cynomolgus monkey islet cells and acinar epithelium in the pancreas) may represent individual variations and/or differences in age, disease status, artifacts of the process of dying, or true species differences. As these sites in the pancreas are not known to express TFPI the observed staining in these tissue elements might represent either previously unrecognized sites of TFPI expression or true tissue cross-reactivity.
L1280: *Reviewer Comments: Tissue cross-reactivity of concizumab was similar between tissue cryosections derived from healthy humans and cynomolgus monkeys.*
L1281: **Table 68. Study Information, Study 209125, Non-GLP**
L1282: Study Features and Methods	Details
GLP compliance	No
Objective	The objective of this study was to evaluate the potential tissue cross reactivity (TCR) of concizumab.
L1283: Study Features and Methods	Details
Methods	
Concizumab was used to stain cryosections of limited normal human and cynomolgus monkey tissues (3 donors per tissue) at concentrations of 1 µg/mL or 10 µg/mL using an indirect precomplexing immunoperoxidase procedure. Positive controls consisted of cryosections of HEK cells expressing human TFPI and HEK cells expressing cynomolgus monkey TFPI. Negative controls consisted of cryosections of HEK cells expressing human interferon alpha and HEK cells expressing cynomolgus monkey interferon alpha. The specific reactivity of concizumab with the positive controls and the lack of specific reactivity with the negative controls, as well as the lack of reactivity of the control IgG with the positive and negative controls, indicated that the assay was sensitive and specific.

Results	
(Human)
Human tissues staining by concizumab was present in the endothelium of small to medium sized vessels in cerebellum, esophagus, small intestine, heart, kidney, liver, lung, lymph node, pancreas, spleen, and thymus. In mononuclear cells in the esophagus, small intestine, kidney, liver, lung, lymph node, spleen, and thymus. In spindle cells in the cerebellum, esophagus, small intestine, heart, kidney, liver, lung, lymph node, pancreas, spleen, and thymus. In the extracellular proteinaceous material in cerebellum, esophagus, small intestine, heart, kidney, liver, lung, lymph node, pancreas, spleen, and thymus.
(Cynomolgus Monkey)
In cynomolgus monkeys, concizumab tissue staining of the same cell types was present in the corresponding tissues with the addition of the cerebrum (endothelium, spindle cells, and extracellular proteinaceous material) and pancreas (mononuclear cells). Staining of islet cells and acinar epithelium in the pancreas and mesothelium in the spleen was observed only in the cynomolgus monkey tissue panel (not in the human tissue panel).
L1284: Source: Reviewer constructed summary table of Study 209125 submitted to the BLA.
L1285: Abbreviations: GLP, good laboratory practices; HEK, human embryonic kidney; IgG, immunoglobulin G; TCR, tissue cross reactivity; TFPI, tissue factor pathway inhibitor
L1286: ## Tissue Cross-Reactivity (TCR) Study of Concizumab with Normal Human and Cynomolgus Monkey Tissues (Study 209280, GLP)
L1287: ## Key Study Findings
L1288: - The concizumab staining pattern was (in general) similar between the human and cynomolgus monkey tissues examined and corresponded to known sites of TFPI tissue expression.
- The noted differences observed between humans and cynomolgus monkeys with regards to concizumab tissue staining patterns (e.g., human decidual cell staining in the placenta and
L1289: testicular spermatogenic cells, placental Hofbauer cells and trophoblastic epithelium in the cynomolgus monkey) may represent either sites with novel TFPI expression or true tissue cross reactivity.
L1290: *Reviewer Comments: With the exception of the positive control HEK-293 cells that overexpressed membrane associated TFPI (either human or cynomolgus monkey), all of the cellular staining observed in this TCR study was localized to the cytoplasm or cytoplasmic structures and these cellular locations are unlikely to be accessible to concizumab under in vivo conditions.*
L1291: **Table 69. Study Information, Study 209280, GLP**
L1292: Study Features and Methods	Details
GLP compliance	Yes
Objective	The objective of this study was to evaluate the potential tissue cross reactivity (TCR) of concizumab with cryosections of normal healthy human and cynomolgus monkey tissues.
Methods	Concizumab was used to stain cryosections of normal human and cynomolgus monkey tissues (3 donors per tissue) at concentrations of 1 µg/mL or 10 µg/mL using an indirect precomplexing immune-peroxidase procedure. Positive controls consisted of cryosections of HEK cells expressing human TFPI and HEK cells expressing cynomolgus monkey TFPI. The negative control consisted of cryosections of non-transfected HEK cells. The specific reactivity of concizumab with the positive controls and the lack of specific reactivity with the negative control, as well as the lack of reactivity of a control IgG with the positive and negative controls, indicated that the assay was sensitive and specific.
Results	
(Human)
Human tissues staining by concizumab was present in the endothelium of small to medium sized vessels, in hematopoietic precursor cells and mononuclear cells, in spindle cells, in extracellular proteinaceous material, and in placental decidual cells.
(Cynomolgus Monkey)
In cynomolgus monkeys, concizumab tissue staining of the same cell types was present with the exception of placental decidual cells. Staining of testicular spermatogenic cells, placental Hofbauer cells (placental villous macrophages) and trophoblastic epithelium was observed only in the cynomolgus monkey tissue panel (not observed in the human tissue panel).
L1293: Source: Reviewer constructed summary table of Study 209280 submitted to the BLA.
L1294: Abbreviations: GLP, good laboratory practices; HEK, human embryonic kidney; IgG, immunoglobulin G; TCR, tissue cross reactivity; TFPI, tissue factor pathway inhibitor
L1295: ## **Concizumab Biodistribution and Immunohistochemistry (IHC) for Endogenous TFPI in the Cynomolgus Monkey Choroid Plexus (Study KAHL130402, non-GLP)**
L1296: ## **Key Study Findings**
L1297: - The intensity of IHC staining for concizumab increased (in a dose-dependent manner) in the choroid plexus of cynomolgus monkeys (more intense staining at higher doses of concizumab)
- Immunohistochemical reactivity for concizumab was predominantly observed in the lumen of blood vessels (suggesting its presence in the blood plasma) and the endothelium
- Immunohistochemical reactivity for concizumab in a sample of inflamed choroid plexus tissue derived from the 26-week cynomolgus monkey study displayed a punctate staining pattern that likely represented granular tissue deposits of concizumab
- Immunohistochemical reactivity for endogenous TFPI was predominantly associated with the endothelium and the intensity of the endothelial TFPI staining was stronger (versus controls) in tissues derived from concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys
- A minimal amount of TFPI staining was observed in the lumen of blood vessels derived from concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys only (not present in controls)
L1298: *Reviewer Comments: Concizumab was predominantly distributed to the plasma and endothelium in tissues derived from the 26-week cynomolgus monkey study. Staining of TFPI was enhanced in tissues derived from concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys and suggests feedback from the coagulation system to increase TFPI levels in response to the loss in TFPI activity in normal cynomolgus monkeys.*
L1299: **Table 70. Study Information, Study KAHL130402, Non-GLP**
L1300: Study Features and Methods	Details
GLP compliance	No
Objective	The objective of this study was to analyze the biodistribution of concizumab and the expression of endogenous TFPI in the choroid plexus of cynomolgus monkeys treated for 26 weeks.
Methods	An immunohistochemical (IHC) stain for detecting concizumab in cynomolgus monkey tissue was established using a monoclonal mouse anti-human IgG4 antibody on paraffin-embedded samples. An IHC stain for detecting endogenous TFPI in cynomolgus monkey tissue was established using a monoclonal mouse anti-human TFPI antibody on paraffin embedded tissues. These IHC stains were applied to tissue samples of choroid plexus from the 26-week toxicity study conducted in cynomolgus monkeys (Study 211087 Section 13.1.2.1).
L1301: Source: Reviewer constructed summary table of Study KAHL130402 submitted to the BLA
L1302: Abbreviations: GLP, good laboratory practices; IHC, immunohistochemical; TFPI, tissue factor pathway inhibitor
L1303: ## **Metabolism**
L1304: Concizumab is a monoclonal antibody (mAb) consisting of naturally occurring (endogenous) amino acids. Hence the metabolism of concizumab is expected to involve the proteolytic digestion of the antibody into peptides and its constituent amino acids. Prototypical biotransformation studies are therefore not considered relevant for concizumab.
L1305: ## **Excretion**
L1306: No dedicated excretion studies were conducted as concizumab is expected to be proteolytically broken down into individual amino acids that will either be repurposed or excreted in vivo.
L1307: ## **Pharmacokinetic Drug Interactions**
L1308: The potential for pharmacokinetic drug-drug interactions between concizumab and rFVIIa was investigated in a 28 Day (PK/PD) Drug-Drug Interaction (Concizumab and rFVIIa) Toxicity Study in Cynomolgus Monkeys (Study 215431). There were no notable differences in rFVIIa exposures (Day 28) in the presence or absence of steady state concizumab and the pharmacokinetics of concizumab was not assessed after the administration of rFVIIa (Day 28). This study was thoroughly reviewed under the PD drug interactions section above (Study 215431 Section 13.1.1.3).

L1342: ## 4 Week Dose Range Finding Subcutaneous Administration Toxicity Study (Including Safety Pharmacology Endpoints) and a 6 Week Subcutaneous and IV Administration Toxicokinetic Study in Cynomolgus Monkeys (Study 208250, Non-GLP)
L1343: ## Key Study Findings
L1344: - There were no toxicologically significant findings following subcutaneous administration of concizumab to cynomolgus monkeys at 2, 20, 80; 20, 80, 160; or 80, 160, 200 mg/kg/day over 4 weeks (3 escalating doses administered on Days 1, 15 and 29).
- Electrocardiograms and blood pressure assessed at 24 hours post-dose (Days 1, 15 and 29) were comparable to pre-treatment and pre-dose values.
L1345: - There was no evidence of pathological clotting in any tissue including the liver, lungs, and brain.
- The time to maximum concentration (T_{max}) was generally observed at 24 to 72 hours (post dose) and bioavailability following (SC) administration was 77%. Concizumab levels exceeded the tolerated amount for detection of antibodies in the anti-drug antibody (ADA) assay.
- The No-Observed Effect Level (NOEL) for a decline in fibrinogen was 2 mg/kg/day (SC) and is indicative of the recruitment of fibrinogen to sites of thrombogenesis.
L1346: *Reviewer Comments: The no observed adverse effect level (NOAEL) was considered at 200 mg/kg/day (SC). Changes in coagulation parameters (consistent with the anticipated pharmacological effects of concizumab) were difficult to interpret in the absence of concurrent control data and the high degree of variability in the data.*
L1347: **Table 76. Study Information, Study 208250, Non-GLP**
L1348: Study Features and Methods	Details
GLP compliance	No
Objective	The objective of this study was to examine preliminary toxicity, safety pharmacology, toxicokinetics, immunogenicity and bioavailability of concizumab following three repeat subcutaneous (SC) dose administrations to cynomolgus monkeys over a 4-week period. The toxicokinetic profile of concizumab was also assessed over a 6-week period following a single subcutaneous or intravenous (IV) administration to male cynomolgus monkeys.
Methods	Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality, clinical observations, body weight, food consumption (qualitative assessment), blood pressure, electrocardiogram (ECG), and clinical and anatomical pathology investigations. Blood samples, for toxicokinetic evaluation, antibody analyses, immunophenotyping, coagulation markers and cytokine analysis were also acquired.
L1349: Source: Reviewer constructed summary table of Study 208250 submitted to the BLA.
L1350: Abbreviations: ECG, electrocardiogram; GLP, good laboratory practices; IV, intravenous; SC, subcutaneous
L1351: ## **4 Week Subcutaneous and Intravenous Administration Toxicity Study in the Cynomolgus Monkey (Study 210130, GLP)**
L1352: ## Key Study Findings
L1353: - Concizumab was pharmacologically active in cynomolgus monkeys as indicated by the observed reduction in circulating levels of fibrinogen (consistent with the recruitment of fibrinogen to sites of thrombogenesis) and increases in D-dimers and TAT (indicative of hemostatic activation), and are consistent with the exaggerated pro-coagulative (antihemorrhagic) effects of concizumab.
- The pro-coagulative effects of concizumab were not associated with any notable changes in PT, APTT, platelets parameters or thrombi formation in normo-coagulative cynomolgus
L1354: monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV).

- Inflammatory cell foci/perivascular cuffing (minimal to moderate) around the blood vessels in the subcutis of the subcutaneous injection site were present in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab at \ge 3 mg/kg/day (SC) and the severity of these findings tended to increase with dose. These findings were not observed in either sex administered 30 mg/kg/week (IV) and suggests that concizumab acts as a local irritant when administered via the SC route.
L1355: *Reviewer Comments: The reviewer agreed with the Applicant proposed NOAELs for the subcutaneous (SC, 9 mg/kg/day) and the intravenous (IV, 30 mg/kg/week) routes of administration (ROA) based on the absence of systemic toxicity in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV). The SC NOAEL (9 mg/kg/day, sex-averaged, 37400 \mug.hr/mL, AUC0-24, Week 4) represents a 1670x exposure margin to the anticipated clinical dose (0.2 mg/kg, 22.4 \mug.hr/mL AUC0-24).*

L1360: **Table 78. Observations and Results, Study 210130, GLP**
L1361: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	There were no unscheduled deaths in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV).
Clinical signs	Soft and/or liquid feces were observed in cynomolgus monkeys in all groups (including IV controls) during the administration period with some individuals presenting with soft feces prior to the initiation of dosing. The cause/source of the GI distress observed in cynomolgus monkeys was not determined.
L1362: Parameters	Major Findings
Body weights and food consumption	There were no notable changes in body weights or qualitative appraisals (visual inspections) of food consumption in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV).
Hematology	(WBC Parameters): White blood cell counts (\uparrow 82\%) and neutrophil counts (\uparrow 91\%) were statistically increased in females (only) administered concizumab at 9 mg/kg/day (SC) on Day 29. This effect was not observed at the lower dose (SC) or at the IV dose (30 mg/kg/week). There were no notable changes in platelet parameters in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV).
Coagulation	Prothrombin Time (PT) and Activated Partial Thromboplastin Time (APTT): There were no notable changes in PT and APTT in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV).
Fibrinogen, D-dimers, and Thrombin/Anti-Thrombin (TAT): A reduction in circulating levels of fibrinogen (consistent with the recruitment of fibrinogen to thrombus sites) and increases in D-dimers and TAT (indicative of hemostatic activation) were observed in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV) and are consistent with the exaggerated pro-coagulative (antihemorrhagic) pharmacological effects of concizumab. These findings were not associated with notable changes in platelet parameters or thrombi formation in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV).
Clinical chemistry	There were no notable changes in clinical chemistry parameters in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV).
Toxicokinetics	The plasma exposure to concizumab tended to be lower in females relative to males with both routes of administration (not statistically significant). Accumulation after once-daily subcutaneous dosing (3 mg/kg/day and 9 mg/kg/day) was extensive (up to 275-fold) and after once-weekly intravenous dosing (30 mg/kg) plasma accumulation was 2-fold to 3-fold. The plasma terminal half-life (t_{1/2}) after intravenous (IV) administration ranged from 122 hrs to 340 hrs (5 days to 14 days), barring one male (#11) in which a t_{1/2} of 24 hrs was observed. This male was excluded from the TK analysis due to the notable reduction in exposure on Day 22 and the minimal exposure noted on Day 29. These changes were likely driven by the formation of neutralizing anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) to concizumab (no ADA analysis was conducted) and correlated with a loss of pharmacodynamic activity in this individual.
Gross pathology	There were no notable changes in tissues derived from cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 4 weeks at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV).
Organ weights	(Spleen): Spleen weight increased significantly (\uparrow 59\%, body weight relative) in females administered 9 mg/kg/day (SC) in the absence of microscopic changes. This finding was not observed in males (SC) or in either sex administered 30 mg/kg/week (IV) and its clinical relevance is unclear.
L1363: Parameters	Major Findings
Histopathology
Adequate Battery: Yes	(Subcutaneous Injection Site): Inflammatory cell foci/perivascular cuffing were present in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab at \ge 3 mg/kg/day (SC) and the severity of these changes increased with dose. This finding was characterized by the presence of perivascular inflammatory cells forming a sheath (cuff) around blood vessels in the subcutis. This finding was not observed in either sex administered 30 mg/kg/week (IV).
L1364: Source: Reviewer constructed summary table of the toxicology Study (210130) submitted to the BLA.
L1365: Abbreviations: ADA, anti-drug antibody; APTT, activated partial thromboplastin time; GI, gastrointestinal; IV, intravenous; PT, prothrombin time; SC, subcutaneous; TAT, thrombin/anti-thrombin; WBC, white blood cells
L1366: ## **13 Week Administration (SC/IV) Toxicity and Safety Pharmacology Study in Cynomolgus Monkeys Followed by a 11 Week Recovery Period (Study 209206, GLP)**
L1367: ## Key Study Findings
L1368: - There was one unscheduled mortality (Day 35) of a MD male (#12) that was deemed related to a gastrointestinal tract lesion that correlated with loose feces and debility (weakness). While it's likely that these findings were unrelated to concizumab dosing it should be noted that this individual presented with a single thrombus in the liver which was considered driven by dehydration but is potentially related to concizumab exposure in view of its pharmacology.
- Concizumab was pharmacologically active in cynomolgus monkeys as indicated by the observed decreases in fibrinogen and increases in Thrombin Antithrombin Complex (TAT) and D-dimers (biomarkers of increased coagulation). Activated partial thromboplastin time (APTT) was prolonged (increased clotting time) early on (Day 16) in males only (in the absence of a decline in platelets) and may be indicative of homeostatic regulation of the coagulation system in normo-coagulative cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab.
- Safety pharmacology endpoints (neurobehavioral, respiratory rate, blood pressure and electrocardiography), immunophenotyping data and a cytokine assessment were all unremarkable in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab at \le 50 mg/kg/day (SC) or 200 mg/kg/week (IV) for 13-weeks. Safety pharmacology endpoints were not assessed post-recovery and there was no indication of delayed toxicity in immunophenotyping or cytokine values.
- Antibodies to concizumab (ADAs) were detected in 90% of control cynomolgus monkeys; however, measurable levels of drug exposure were not observed, and the Applicant did not provide an explanation for the presence of ADAs in the control group. The Applicant's anti-drug antibody (ADA) assessment was likely insufficient to assess the extent of ADA production in the presence of high plasma concentrations of concizumab (due to assay interference). When ADAs were detected, their presence correlated with a decline in pharmacodynamic activity (e.g., fibrinogen, TAT, and D-dimers) and a reduction in systemic exposure, indicating that the ADAs produced in cynomolgus monkeys (particularly at the LD) were inhibitory in nature.
- Adverse changes in the vasculature (e.g., heart and lung thrombosis and pulmonary vascular intimal proliferation, chronic inflammation, hemorrhage, and blood vessel
L1369: necrosis/mineralization) were observed in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (50 mg/kg/day, SC and/or 200 mg/kg/week, IV) and thrombosis persisted in the heart and lung following the 11-week recovery period. Thrombosis was likely the result of exaggerated pharmacology and was considered adverse in cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state as their presence impacted local blood circulation in the individuals affected and failed to normalize post recovery.
L1370: - Chronic inflammation and subendothelial intimal proliferation in the lung were likely driven by the observed immunogenic response (deposition of immune complexes, secondary to development of ADAs) to concizumab (a humanized mAb) in cynomolgus monkeys and resolved during the 11-week recovery period.
- Inflammatory cell foci/perivascular cuffing were present in the subcutaneous injection site in all groups (including controls). However, the overall incidence and severity tended to increase in HD males (SC) and in females at all doses (\ge 1 mg/kg/day, SC). These findings were characterized by the presence of perivascular inflammatory cells (predominantly mononuclear) forming a sheath (cuff) around blood vessels in the subcutis and were trending towards resolution (minimal severity) in both sexes (MD/HD) following the 11-week recovery period.
- Cellulitis/fibrosis was noted predominantly in the subcutaneous injection site of females (dose independent) at all doses (\ge 1 mg/kg/day) and was characterized by mixed inflammatory cell infiltration into the subcutis, with varying levels of fibroblastic proliferation and collagen deposition. The extent of these findings was comparable to controls in males (SC) and this condition had completely resolved by the end of the 11-week recovery period.
L1371: *Reviewer Comments: The reviewer agreed with the Applicant proposed NOAEL (1 mg/kg/day [LD], SC) based in the adverse changes in the vasculature (e.g., thrombosis) observed at the MD/HD that persisted at the HD following a 11-week recovery period in cynomolgus monkeys administered (SC) concizumab at 1, 10 and 50 mg/kg/day for 13-weeks. A NOAEL for intravenous (IV) dosing was not established as thrombosis-related histopathological findings were observed at the 200 mg/kg/week (IV) dose. The NOAEL (1 mg/kg/day, SC, 5923 \mug.hr/mL AUC0-24, Day 91) represents a 264x exposure margin to the anticipated clinical dose (0.2 mg/kg, 22.4 \mug.hr/mL AUC0-24).*
