L1376: **Table 80. Observations and Results, Study 209206, GLP**
L1377: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	
There was one unscheduled death at the 10 mg/kg/day (MD, SC) dose (male #12) where this individual was terminated on Day 35 due to poor condition. Prior to termination, this male presented with intermittent body weight loss (up to ↓11%, Day -7 to Day 29), diarrhea (Week 1 to Week 2) and was thin and dehydrated up until the day of its sacrifice.
Predose clinical pathology samples revealed changes in kidney and liver parameters, notably low plasma electrolytes (correlating with a gastrointestinal tract lesion and loose feces) and elevated plasma urea and creatinine. Dosing in this male was intermittent, and this individual was treated with intravenous fluid therapy as well as antibiotics. The observed clinical signs were likely unrelated to the administration of concizumab as the body weight loss commenced prior to the initiation of dosing.
The cause of death in this male was considered related to the gastrointestinal tract lesion, which correlated with the loose feces and debility (weakness) recorded clinically. While it's unlikely that these findings were related to concizumab dosing it should be noted that this male presented with a single thrombus in the liver which was deemed related to the dehydration but is consistent with the pharmacology of concizumab.

Clinical signs	
Gastrointestinal distress (soft and/or liquid feces) was present in all groups (including controls) with some individuals displaying these signs prior to the commencement of dosing. A single LD (1 mg/kg/day, SC) female (#27) and 2 MD (10 mg/kg/day, SC) monkeys (#9, male and #33, female) were thin and/or dehydrated at various times within the first 4 weeks of the administration period. There was no clear dose-response relationship and the Applicant failed to identify the root cause of these clinical signs.
L1378: Parameters	Major Findings
At the subcutaneous injection site there were intermittent incidences of rash/redness in all groups (including controls) and these local skin reactions were deemed to be related to the dosing procedure.

Body weights and food consumption	
Minimal body weight loss (Week 8 to Week 13) was observed at the 200 mg/kg/week dose (IV) and by the end of the administration period male body weight was reduced (\downarrow9%) relative to controls. Female body weights were comparable to controls at the end of dosing and there were no effects on body weights in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab subcutaneously (\le 50 mg/kg/day). Following the 11-week recovery period, body weights were comparable to controls indicating that this condition was reversible in males and there was no indication of delayed toxicity in other groups. Qualitative assessments (visual inspections) did not reveal any corresponding changes in food consumption.

Ophthalmoscopy	Unremarkable
Hematology	
There were no notable changes in RBC, WBC or platelet parameters derived from cynomolgus monkeys dosed with concizumab [\le 50 mg/kg/day (SC) or 200 mg/kg/week (IV)] for 13 weeks and there was no evidence of delayed hematologic toxicity.

Immunophenotyping	
There were no definitive changes observed during assessments of white blood cell (WBC) sub-sets (performed by flow cytometry) in the following WBC populations: total T lymphocytes (CD3+), total B lymphocytes (CD20+) and total monocytes (CD14+) derived from cynomolgus monkeys dosed with concizumab [\le 50 mg/kg/day (SC) or 200 mg/kg/week (IV)] for 13 weeks and there was no evidence of a delayed onset of immune cell toxicity.

Coagulation	
(Prothrombin Time, PT): There were no clear changes in PT values derived from cynomolgus monkeys dosed with concizumab [\le 50 mg/kg/day (SC) or 200 mg/kg/week (IV)] for 13 weeks and there was no evidence of a delayed onset of toxicity.
(Activated Partial Thromboplastin Time, APTT): Activated partial thromboplastin times were prolonged in males at 24 hrs after the administration (Day 16) of concizumab on Day 15 (\uparrow28%, \uparrow44% and \uparrow43% at the LD, MD, and HD, respectively (SC) and \uparrow68% at the 200 mg/kg/week dose, IV). These changes occurred in the absence of changes in platelet parameters and there were no significant changes in APTT during the remainder of the administration period in males (Days 30, 58, 92 and 93). This effect was not observed in females at any point during the study and there was no evidence of a delayed onset of toxicity in either sex. Activated partial thromboplastin time prolongation (increased clotting time) is counterintuitive to the anticipated effects of an antihemorrhagic (that seeks to decrease the time needed to induce clotting) and may be indicative of homeostatic regulation of the coagulation system in cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state.
(Fibrinogen): Fibrinogen concentrations tended to decline independent of dose (SC and IV) level (intermittently significant) from Day 16 forward in females (\le \downarrow31%) and from Day 30 forward
L1379: Parameters	Major Findings
in males (\le ↓30%) and is consistent with the recruitment of fibrinogen to sites of thrombogenesis. Fibrinogen levels tended to normalize during the recovery period (Day 169).
(D-dimer Biomarker): D-dimer is a fibrin degradation product (a small protein fragment present in the blood after a blood clot is degraded by fibrinolysis) and it is so named because it contains two D fragments of the fibrin protein joined by a cross-link, hence forming a protein dimer. D-dimers are not normally present in blood plasma and tend to manifest when the coagulation system has been activated. The concentrations of D-dimer increased (in general, dose-dependently) and were significantly higher on Day 16 (up to ↑5-fold, females), Day 30 (up to ↑5-fold, males), Day 58 (up to ↑6-fold, females), and Day 92 (up to ↑7-fold and ↑5-fold, in males and females, respectively). Elevations in D-dimer levels tended to persist through the end of recovery (both sexes) at doses \ge 10 mg/kg (SC and IV) indicating that the 11-week recovery period was insufficient to restore normal coagulation in cynomolgus monkeys previously administered concizumab at these levels.
(Thrombin Antithrombin (TAT) Complex): Thrombin antithrombin complex is a protein complex of thrombin and antithrombin and is a marker of net activation of coagulation. Thrombin antithrombin complex (TAT) concentrations increased with dose in both sexes on Day 16 and were found to be statistically significant in males (\le ↑2-fold, SC). Consistent with the observed increases in D-dimer levels, elevations in TAT tended to persist through the end of recovery (both sexes) at doses \ge 10 mg/kg (SC and IV) indicating that the 11-week recovery period was insufficient to restore normal coagulation in cynomolgus monkeys previous administered concizumab at these levels.

Clinical chemistry	
There were no clear changes in clinical chemistry parameters in whole blood derived from cynomolgus monkeys dosed with concizumab [\le 50 mg/kg/day (SC) or 200 mg/kg/week (IV)] for 13 weeks and there was no evidence of a delayed onset of toxicity.

Cytokine assessment	
There were no definitive changes in cytokine (IL-2, IL-4, IL-5, IL-6, TNF-alpha and IFN-gamma) levels during assessments (bead-based flow cytometry) of serum derived from cynomolgus monkeys dosed with concizumab [\le 50 mg/kg/day (SC) or 200 mg/kg/week (IV)] for 13 weeks and there was no evidence of a delayed onset of toxicity.

Anti-drug antibody (ADA) analysis	
Antibodies to concizumab (ADAs) were detected in 9 of the 10 cynomolgus monkeys in the control group; however, measurable levels of drug exposure were not observed (based on toxicokinetic assessment). The Applicant did not provide an explanation for the antibody results in the control group.
At the end of the main study period, ADAs were detected in 2 of 9 cynomolgus monkeys in the low dose group (1 mg/kg/day, SC) and at the end of the recovery period ADAs were detected in the low and intermediate dose groups (1 and 10 mg/kg/day; 4/4 and 1/3 cynomolgus monkeys, respectively). The study report stated that the ADA assay could tolerate up to 100 \mug/mL concizumab in
L1380: Parameters	Major Findings
samples and still detect 1000 ng/mL ADAs. Mean drug concentrations exceeded the ADA assay tolerance (100 µg/mL) at the 50 mg/kg/day dose (SC) on Day 1 and at all doses (SC and IV) on Day 91. Hence, the Applicant's anti-drug antibody (ADA) assessment was likely insufficient to assess the extent of ADA production in the presence of high plasma concentrations of concizumab (due to assay interference).

Toxicokinetics	
The plasma terminal half-life (t_{1/2}) for concizumab was, in general, short at the low dose (70 hrs to 80 hrs) and was extended at doses \ge 10 mg/kg (400 hrs to 500 hrs). Accumulation in systemic exposure to concizumab after (SC) dosing was extensive (up to 1000-fold) and after once weekly (IV) dosing at 200 mg/kg, plasma accumulation was 2-fold to 4-fold.
Cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab once daily (SC) or once weekly (IV) for 3 months were systemically exposed throughout the administration period, with the exception of two LD individuals that displayed no exposure after nearly two months of dosing (Day 58). Cynomolgus monkeys dosed at \ge 10 mg/kg/day (SC), or 200 mg/kg/week (IV) were likely exposed throughout the 11-week recovery period

Gross pathology	
There were no notable changes in tissues derived from cynomolgus monkeys dosed with concizumab [\le 50 mg/kg/day (SC) or 200 mg/kg/week (IV)] for 13 weeks and there was no evidence of a delayed onset of toxicity.

Organ weights	Unremarkable
Histopathology	
(Heart): Histopathological evaluations revealed thrombosis in the heart (a mature thrombus was present in the right ventricle of 1 HD female (#38) administered (SC) concizumab at 50 mg/kg/day. There were no notable findings in the heart at doses \le 10 mg/kg/day. A mature thrombus in the left ventricle of the heart was present in 1 HD (SC) female (#40) following the 11-week recovery period suggesting non-reversibility and/or delay onset.
(Lungs): Thrombus was present in the lumen of the lung (small and medium sized blood vessels) where the vessels exhibited vascular intimal proliferation in 1 HD female (#39) administered (SC) concizumab at 50 mg/kg/day. This female also presented with chronic inflammation of the lung (left posterior lobe) that was characterized as a focal area of lung with fibroblastic proliferation and hemorrhage, and blood vessels with necrotic/mineralized walls. Vascular intimal proliferation in the lung manifested at the 200 mg/kg/week (IV) dose in the posterior lung lobe in 1 male (#19) and 1 female (#42) and was characterized by multi-layering and infolding of the vascular endothelium, and endothelial cells with plump nuclei and increased levels of cytoplasm. A thrombus (medium sized artery) was present in the female (#42, IV) and there were no notable findings in the lungs at doses \le 10 mg/kg/day (SC).
An early fibrin thrombus was present in a large artery of the posterior lung lobe in 1 HD (SC) female (#40) following the 11-week recovery period (Note: this female also presented with a mature thrombus in the left ventricle of the heart, post recovery).

Adequate battery: yes
L1381: Parameters	Major Findings
The chronic inflammation and vascular intimal proliferation in the lung were not observed after the 11-week recovery period.
(Injection Site): Subcutaneous inflammatory cell foci/perivascular cuffing was present in all groups (including controls). The overall incidence and severity tended to increase in HD males (SC) and in females at all doses (\ge 1 mg/kg/day, SC) and these findings were characterized by the presence of perivascular inflammatory cells (predominantly mononuclear) forming a sheath (cuff) around blood vessels in the subcutis. This finding was trending towards resolution (minimal severity) in both sexes (MD/HD) following the 11-week recovery period.
Subcutaneous cellulitis/fibrosis was noted predominantly in females (dose independent) at all doses (\ge 1 mg/kg/day, SC) and was characterized by mixed inflammatory cell infiltration into the subcutis, with varying levels of fibroblastic proliferation and collagen deposition. The extent of these findings was comparable to controls in males (SC) and there were no notable findings in the intravenous injection site of either sex at the end of the administration period. This condition had completely resolved by the end of the 11-week recovery period.

L1384: ## **26 Week Toxicity Study by the Subcutaneous and Intravenous Routes of Administration in Cynomolgus Monkeys With a 13 Week Recovery Period (Study 211087, GLP)**
L1385: ## Key Study Findings
L1386: - A NOAEL was not identified in the 26-week repeat-dose toxicity study conducted in cynomolgus monkeys due to the presence of thrombi and/or vascular changes at all doses. The material assessed in the 26-week study contained a disproportionate increase in the level of high molecular weight proteins (HMWPs) that consisted predominantly of concizumab multimers. In contrast, the material used in the 4-, 13-, and 52-week toxicity studies was comparable to the clinical material and contained significantly lower amounts of HMWPs. Evaluations were conducted to assess if the change in impurity profile could account for the increased toxicity observed in the 26-week cynomolgus monkey study. There were no significant differences noted between the monomeric and the multimeric forms of concizumab across multiple in vitro assays (See Section 13.1.2.5.3 Mechanistic Analyses). The contribution of the multimeric forms of concizumab to the observed pathology/toxicity in the 26-week cynomolgus monkey study was considered driven by increased immunogenicity and the subsequent immune complex (IC) formation/deposition.

- There was one unscheduled mortality at the 9 mg/kg/day (SC) dose (HD) that was triggered by the onset of a severe adverse reaction (Day 121) to concizumab (0.5 hours post-dose) in a single female (#34) where thrombosis and subsequent massive coagulative lung necrosis precipitated the moribund condition.
L1387: - Concizumab was pharmacologically active in cynomolgus monkeys as indicated by the observed decreases in TFPI activity and fibrinogen and the increases in D-dimer and TAT (biomarkers of increased coagulation). Prothrombin time and activated partial thromboplastin time were prolonged (in the absence of a significant decline in platelets) and may be indicative of homeostatic regulation of coagulation in normo-coagulative monkeys administered an antihemorrhagic.
- The Applicant's anti-drug antibody (ADA) assessment in cynomolgus monkeys was likely insufficient to assess the extent of ADA production in the presence of high plasma concentrations of concizumab (due to assay interference). In some cases, the development of ADAs correlated with a reduction in systemic exposure and suggests that the ADAs produced in cynomolgus monkeys were likely inhibitory in nature.
- Adverse changes in the vasculature of the brain and/or choroid plexus (e.g., hypertrophy, hyperplasia, inflammation, edema and/or lymphoid foci) and the lungs (e.g., subendothelial intimal thickening and/or endothelial cell hypertrophy) were observed in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV) for 26 weeks. These vascular changes are consistent with an immunogenic response (deposition of immune complexes, secondary to development of ADAs) to a foreign protein and the administration of an antihemorrhagic (pro-coagulant) to cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state and tended to resolve during recovery.
- Thrombosis manifested in the lungs, liver, epididymis, and thymus of cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV) for 26 weeks. Thrombi were present in the lungs, heart, and IV injection site at the end of recovery and indicate that a duration of 13 weeks was insufficient to reverse the presence of drug-induced thrombi in normo-coagulative monkeys previously administered concizumab at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV). Thrombosis was likely the result of exaggerated pharmacology and was considered adverse at all doses in cynomolgus monkeys.
- Perivascular cellular infiltrates (consisting predominantly of plasma cells and lymphocytes, e.g., neutrophils, eosinophils, and giant cells) and endothelial hypertrophy in the local blood vessels were associated with the subcutaneous injection site and were trending towards resolution post-recovery (13 weeks) in cynomolgus monkeys previously administered concizumab at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV) for 26 weeks.
L1388: *Reviewer Comments: The reviewer agreed with the Applicant that a NOAEL could not be established for this study based in the adverse changes in the vasculature and thrombosis observed in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) and 30 mg/kg/week (IV) for 26-weeks. The lowest observed adverse effect level (LOAEL) for subcutaneous (SC) dosing was 3 mg/kg/day (21350 \mug.hr/mL, AUC0-24, sex-averaged, Week 26) and represents a 953x exposure margin to the anticipated clinical dose (0.2 mg/kg/day, 22.4 \mug.hr/mL AUC0-24).*

L1393: **Table 82. Observations and Results, Study 211087, GLP**
L1394: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	
There was one unscheduled death (female #34) at the 9 mg/kg/day (SC) dose (HD) where this individual was terminated on Day 121 due to the onset of a severe adverse reaction to concizumab. Clinical signs in this HD female following the administration of concizumab (Day 121) included: severe subdued behavior, paleness (gums and face), shallow breathing, slow movement, and a collapsed state (unconsciousness). Due to the moribund condition, this female was terminated at 0.5 hrs post-dose on Day 121. There were no clinical signs recorded in this female (#34) before Day 121.
Hematological values (versus predose) in this female showed a marked reduction in hemoglobin concentrations, erythrocyte counts and hematocrit that corresponded to a notable increase in reticulocyte counts and elevations in total leukocyte, and neutrophil counts. Coagulation parameter values were comparable to predose and no dose controls. The Applicant indicated that (at the time of termination) this female had presented with a heavy menstrual blood flow that was observed for 9 days and may have contributed to the observed anemia.
Clinical chemistry values (versus predose) in this HD female showed increases in creatine phosphokinase activity, creatinine and phosphate concentrations and reductions in sodium, chloride, total protein, albumin, and globulin concentrations.
L1395: Parameters	Major Findings
Pale discoloration of the internal organs was observed at necropsy and a recently formed blood clot was present in the vagina that correlated clinically and histologically with menstruation. Two firm, mottled masses were present in the caudal lung lobes and upon histopathological evaluation, thrombosis and subsequent massive coagulative necrosis of the lung parenchyma were determined to be the cause of the moribund condition. In addition, subendothelial intimal thickening manifested in a number of blood vessels, and a subset of affected blood vessels showed an accumulation of foamy macrophages within the thickened intimal stroma. Endothelial hypertrophy and hyperplasia were also observed in blood vessels in the lungs and mononuclear cell infiltration (minimal) was present in the choroid plexus.

Clinical signs	
Barring the adverse clinical signs observed in the HD female (#37) that was terminated early (Day 121; Week 18), there were no notable clinical signs associated with the administration of concizumab at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV) to cynomolgus monkeys for 26-weeks.

Body weights and food consumption	
There were no notable differences in the observed trends in body weight changes or the qualitative measurement of food consumption in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\le 9 mg/kg/day, SC or 30 mg/kg/week, IV) for 26-weeks.

Ophthalmoscopy	
Unremarkable

Hematology	
Barring the hematological changes observed in the HD female (#37) that was terminated early (Day 121; Week 18), there were no notable changes in hematological parameters (including platelet parameters) in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\le 9 mg/kg/day, SC or 30 mg/kg/week, IV) for 26-weeks or following the 13-week recovery period

Coagulation	
(Prothrombin Time, PT) Prothrombin time prolongation (significant) was observed in intravenously dosed males only (30 mg/kg/week, IV) from Week 6 forward and tended to normalize during the recovery period. Prothrombin time prolongation (increased clotting time) is counterintuitive to the anticipated effects of an antihemorrhagic (that seeks to decrease the time needed to induce clotting) and may be indicative of homeostatic regulation of the coagulation system in cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state.
(Activated Partial Thromboplastin Time, APTT) Activated partial thromboplastin time prolongation (significant intermittently) manifested during Week 6 in intravenously dosed males (30 mg/kg/week, IV) and in LD (3 mg/kg/day, SC) females during Week 13 and LD/HD (\le 9 mg/kg/day, SC) females during Week 26. These findings tended to normalize during the recovery period. Activated partial thromboplastin time prolongation (increased clotting time) is also counterintuitive to the anticipated effects of an antihemorrhagic (that seeks to decrease the time needed to induce clotting) and may be indicative of homeostatic regulation of the coagulation system in cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state.
L1396: Parameters	Major Findings
(Fibrinogen - Prothrombin Time-Derived): Fibrinogen concentrations tended to decline independent of dose level (intermittently significant) from Week 6 forward in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\le 9 mg/kg/day, SC or 30 mg/kg/week, IV) for 26-weeks and is consistent with the recruitment of fibrinogen to sites of clot formation. Fibrinogen concentrations were comparable to pre-dose controls in recovery females but tended to remain lower (not significant) in males following the 13-week recovery period.
(Fibrinogen - Clauss Assay): The Clauss fibrinogen assay is a quantitative, clot-based, functional assay that measures the ability of fibrinogen to form fibrin clot after being exposed to a high concentration of purified thrombin. Fibrinogen concentrations tended to decline (intermittently significant) from Week 6 forward in males administered concizumab (30 mg/kg/week, IV). Similar findings were observed during Week 13 in LD males (3 mg/kg/day, SC) and to a lesser extent in HD males (9 mg/kg/day, SC). Numerical decreases were noted intermittently in females (not significant) and these changes tended to normalize in both sexes during recovery.
(D-dimer Biomarker): D-dimer is a fibrin degradation product (a small protein fragment present in the blood after a blood clot is degraded by fibrinolysis) that is so named because it contains two D fragments of the fibrin protein joined by a cross-link, hence forming a protein dimer. D-dimers are not normally present in blood plasma and tend to manifest when the coagulation system has been activated. The concentrations of D-dimer increased from Week 6 forward in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\le 9 mg/kg/day, SC or 30 mg/kg/week, IV) for 26-weeks and is consistent with the clot promoting effects of concizumab. This effect was persistent at the 9 mg/kg/day (SC) dose and was tending towards normalization at the 3 mg/kg/day (SC) dose and the 30 mg/kg/week (IV) dose.
(Thrombin Antithrombin (TAT) Complex): Thrombin antithrombin complex (TAT) is a protein complex of thrombin and antithrombin and is a marker of net activation of coagulation. Thrombin antithrombin complex (TAT) concentrations tended to increase (intermittently) with dose during the administration period but showed a high degree of intra- and inter-group variability throughout the administration period and following recovery in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\le 9 mg/kg/day, SC or 30 mg/kg/week, IV) for 26-weeks.

Clinical chemistry	
There were no notable changes in clinical chemistry parameters in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\le 9 mg/kg/day, SC or 30 mg/kg/week, IV) for 26-weeks or following the 13-week recovery period.

Residual TFPI functionality (activity)	
Blood samples for the chromogenic plasma TFPI activity were collected on Day -21, Week 1, 5, 9, 13, 17, 21, 25, and 26 (predose) from cynomolgus monkeys. A high degree of variability was observed in the chromogenic activity assay that attempted to quantitate the residual TFPI functionality/activity in the plasma of cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab. Assay values

(U/mL) tended to be lower in concizumab-dosed groups (when adjusted for individual baseline levels) and suggests that the plasma TFPI activity may have been reduced by concizumab.
Anti-drug antibody (ADA) analysis	Two males and two females administered 3 mg/kg/day (SC), 1 male (9 mg/kg/day, SC) and 1 male and 1 female dosed at 30 mg/kg/week (IV) developed anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) to concizumab.
Toxicokinetics	Exposure to concizumab was reduced in two females (#30, 3 mg/kg/day, SC and #36, 30 mg/kg/week, IV) that developed ADAs. In the subcutaneously dose female (#30), exposure was reduced from Week 5 forward and in the female (#36) administered 30 mg/kg/week (IV) the exposure was reduced from Week 13 forward indicating that the ADAs produced in these individuals were likely neutralizing.
In general, lower exposures were observed in females relative to males after subcutaneous administration. Accumulation ratios (R_{\text{acobs}}) ranged from: 60 to 195 at 3 mg/kg/day (SC), 29 to 115 at 9 mg/kg/day (SC) and 1.5 to 5.8 at 30 mg/kg/week (IV).
Urinalysis	Unremarkable

There were no other notable findings observed grossly in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\le 9 mg/kg/day, SC or 30 mg/kg/week, IV) for 26-weeks or following the 13-week recovery period.
Organ weights	Unremarkable
Histopathology	(Brain): Vascular changes (hypertrophy/hyperplasia, inflammation, edema and/or lymphoid foci) in the brain (choroid plexus) were observed in 1 male (#16) administered concizumab at 30 mg/kg (QW) intravenously (IV) and in 1 female (#32, 9 mg/kg/day, SC), 2 males (#7M and #8, 3 mg/kg/day, SC) and 1 female (#26, 3 mg/kg/day, SC) administered concizumab for 26-weeks. Vascular changes in the choroid plexus were not present after the 13-week recovery period. These vascular changes are consistent with an immunogenic response to a foreign protein and the administration of a pro-coagulant (antihemorrhagic) to cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state. A granuloma was noted in the caudate nucleus of the brain of 1 female (#35, 9 mg/kg/day, SC) following the 13-week recovery period and the relationship of this finding to the administration of concizumab is unclear.
(Lung): Vascular changes (subendothelial intimal thickening and/or endothelia cell hypertrophy) in the lungs were observed in 2 males (#17 and #18) administered concizumab at 30 mg/kg (QW) intravenously (IV), 1 female (#33, 9 mg/kg/day, SC) and/or 1 male (#6, 3 mg/kg/day, SC) administered concizumab for 26-weeks. These changes are consistent with those observed in the female (#34) that was euthanized early due to moribundity on Day 121. Vascular changes in the lungs were not observed after the 13-week recovery period. These vascular changes are consistent with an immunogenic response to a foreign protein and the
Adequate battery: yes
L1398: Parameters	Major Findings
administration of an antihemorrhagic (pro-coagulant) to cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state.
(Thrombi): Concizumab induced thrombus formation in the lungs, liver, epididymis, and thymus after 26 weeks and was associated with coagulative necrosis of the lung in 1 female (#27, 9 mg/kg/day, SC). Thrombi were present in the lungs, heart, and injection site (IV) post-recovery and indicates that 13 weeks is insufficient to reverse and/or clear thrombi formation in normo-coagulative monkeys previously administered concizumab at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV).
(Injection Site): Perivascular cellular infiltrates (comprised of plasma cells and lymphocytes, e.g., neutrophils, eosinophils, and giant cells) and endothelial hypertrophy of the blood vessels were associated with the subcutaneous injection site. (Kidney): Kidney sclerosis and tubular degeneration (loss) were noted microscopically (unilaterally) in 1 male (#14, 9 mg/kg/day, SC) following the 13-week recovery period and the significance of this finding is unknown.
(Spleen): Hyalinized germinal centers were present in the spleen of 1 male (#16) administered concizumab at 30 mg/kg/week (IV) for 26-weeks and was consistent with the heightened immune response (tissue inflammation in multiple tissues, including brain) noted in this individual.

L1401: ## **52-Week Toxicity Study by the Subcutaneous Route of Administration in Cynomolgus Monkeys with No Assessment of Recovery (Study 213542, GLP)**
L1402: ## **Key Study Findings**
L1403: - Concizumab was pharmacologically active in cynomolgus monkeys as indicated by the observed decreases in free TFPI, fibrinogen and platelets, and the increase in D-dimer (a biomarker of increased coagulation). Prothrombin time and activated partial thromboplastin time were prolonged (increased clotting time) and may be indicative of homeostatic regulation of the coagulation system in normo-coagulative cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab.
- The Applicant's anti-drug antibody (ADA) assessment in cynomolgus monkeys was likely insufficient to assess the extent of ADA production in the presence of high plasma concentrations of concizumab (due to assay interference). The development of ADAs correlated with a decline in pharmacodynamic activity (e.g., Protein C and platelets), an increase in chromogenic (TFPI) activity and a reduction in systemic exposure, indicating that the ADAs produced in cynomolgus monkeys were inhibitory in nature.
- Adverse changes in the vasculature (e.g., thrombosis, infarction/necrosis and subendothelial intimal thickening) of multiple organ systems were observed in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\ge 1 mg/kg/day, MD) for 13, 26 and 52 Weeks, and the reversibility of these conditions was not assessed. These findings are consistent with the administration of an antihemorrhagic (pro-coagulant) to cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state.
- Thrombosis manifested in the lungs and liver at the HD (9 mg/kg/day) after 13, 26 and 52 weeks, and at the MD (1 mg/kg/day) after 52 weeks. Thrombosis was also present in the heart and axillary lymph nodes at the HD after 13 weeks, in the spleen at the HD after 26 and 52 weeks, in the epididymis and heart at the HD and in the mesenteric lymph nodes at the MD after 52 weeks. Thrombosis at the MD/HD were likely the result of exaggerated pharmacology and were considered adverse in cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state as their presence impacted local blood circulation in the individuals affected.
- Subendothelial intimal thickening of the blood vessels (small to medium-sized arteries and/or veins) in the lungs was observed at the HD after 13, 26 and 52 weeks and at the MD after 52 weeks. This condition was also present in the liver at the HD after 26 weeks and was initially reported to be present in the large intestine (colon) after 13 weeks in the interim study report but, was ultimately excluded from the final study report. The subendothelial intimal thickening was likely driven by the observed immunogenic response (deposition of immune complexes, secondary to development of ADAs) to concizumab (a humanized mAb) in cynomolgus monkeys.
- In the central nervous system (CNS), minimal to marked inflammation (lymphoplasmacytic infiltration, with or without associated eosinophils and macrophages) of the choroid plexus (brain) was observed at the HD after 13 weeks and at all dose levels (\ge 0.5 mg/kg/day) after 26 and 52 weeks. and was likely related to the deposition of immune complexes (ADAs) in cynomolgus monkeys.
L1404: - Lymphoplasmacytic infiltrations were also present at the subcutaneous injection site at the HD after 13 weeks and at all dose levels (\ge 0.5 mg/kg/day) after 26 and 52 weeks, and were consistent with the findings observed in the CNS.
L1405: Reviewer Comments: The reviewer agreed with the Applicant proposed NOAEL (0.5 mg/kg/day, LD) based in the adverse changes in the vasculature (e.g., thrombosis, infarction, subendothelial intimal thickening) and the increased severity in CNS inflammation observed at the MD/HD in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab at 0.5, 1, or 9 mg/kg/day for 52 weeks. The reversibility of the findings in cynomolgus monkeys was not assessed and the NOAEL (0.5 mg/kg/day, 1660 \mug.hr/mL AUC0-24, Week 52) represents a 74x exposure margin to the anticipated clinical dose (0.20 mg/kg, 22.4 \mug.hr/mL AUC0-24).

L1410: **Table 84. Observations and Results, Study 213532, GLP**
L1411: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	A single LD male (#2009) was euthanized during Week 20 (Day 140) due to extensive lacerated wounds to the back sustained during a fight with cage mates. The same male had sustained fight wounds to the left side of the chest (Week 13) and to the thoracic-lumbar region of the back (Week 19) previously. There were no vascular findings or findings in the choroid plexus of the brain during necropsy and the early euthanization of this LD male was considered unrelated to the administration of concizumab.
Clinical signs	There were no notable changes in clinical signs in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\le 9 mg/kg/day) for 52-weeks.
Body weights and food consumption	There were no differences in observed trends in body weight changes or the level of food consumption in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\le 9 mg/kg/day) for 52-weeks.
Ophthalmoscopy	Unremarkable
L1412: Parameters	Major Findings
Electrocardiograms (ECG)	
Electrocardiograms (ECG) were recorded in non-fasted, cynomolgus monkeys (unrestrained except for during the dosing procedure) via a jacketed external telemetry system (lead II ECG waveform morphology) once during pretreatment and at Week 12, 25 and 51 for 20 hrs to 24 hrs (post-dose). Measurement of RR (heart rate), PR, QRS and QT interval values were performed. A distribution-based rate-corrected QT (QTca) was calculated (Note: due to missing baseline data at the HD (9 mg/kg/day), a species-specific QT correction was applied instead of an individual animal correction; however, due to a lack of dose-relationship in the HR and QT interval the study cardiologist considered it unlikely that the QTca was affected).
Significant changes in ECG parameters (Week 12, Week 25, and Week 51) were noted in males and/or females intermittently (at all doses) and in view of statistically significant changes present in some of the baseline data and the lack of a clear dose relationship, the study cardiologist considered these findings to be biologically irrelevant.

Hematology	
(Platelet Parameters): A significant decline in platelet counts was observed in males at all doses, with the effect at the HD (\downarrow44% to \downarrow86%) manifesting by Week 6 and continuing until the end of dosing. The decline in platelets in LD/MD males (\downarrow67% to \downarrow79%) started during Week 34 and continued to the end of dosing. A similar effect was observed in HD females where platelet counts declined (\downarrow58% to \downarrow62%) from Week 44 forward. A significant effect on platelet counts was not observed in MD females, however platelet counts declined significantly in LD females (\downarrow67% to \downarrow73%). The decline in circulating platelet counts is consistent with the recruitment of platelets to thrombus sites.
L1413: Coagulation	
(Prothrombin Time - PT): Significant prothrombin time prolongation (up to \uparrow 17\%) manifested during Week 6 at the HD in both sexes and persisted thru Week 26. This effect tended to wane by Week 30 in both sexes and PT values in HD males were comparable to controls by Week 48. Prothrombin time prolongation (increased clotting time) is counterintuitive to the anticipated effects of an antihemorrhagic (that seeks to decrease the time needed to induce clotting) and may be indicative of homeostatic regulation of the coagulation system in cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state.
(Activated Partial Thromboplastin Time - APTT): Significant activated partial thromboplastin time prolongation (up to \uparrow 16\%) manifested during Week 9 and persisted thru Week 48 in HD males. This effect was present to a lesser extent in HD females during Week 17 (up to \uparrow 7\%). Activated partial thromboplastin time prolongation (increased clotting time) is also counterintuitive to the anticipated effects of an antihemorrhagic (that seeks to decrease the time needed to induce clotting) and may be indicative of homeostatic regulation of the coagulation system in cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state.
(Fibrinogen - Prothrombin Time-Derived): Fibrinogen concentrations tended to decline independent of dose level (intermittently significant) from Week 6 forward in males (\downarrow 41\% to \downarrow 102\%) and females (\downarrow 60\% to \downarrow 109\%) and is consistent with the recruitment of fibrinogen to thrombus sites.
(D-dimer Biomarker): The concentrations of D-dimer increased (dose-dependently) and were notably higher at Week 13, 26, 39 and 52 in MD/HD monkeys. These changes tended to be statistically significant at the HD after Week 13 (excluding females at Week 52) and at the MD during Weeks 26 and/or 39. The increase in D-dimer is consistent with the clot promoting effects of concizumab.
(Protein C (Autoprothrombin IIA) Biomarker): Protein C (a natural anticoagulant) is a vitamin K-dependent glycoprotein structurally similar to other vitamin K-dependent proteins affecting blood clotting (i.e., prothrombin, Factor VII, Factor IX and Factor X). The Protein C system provides important control of blood coagulation by regulating the activities of factor VIIIa (FVIIIa) and factor Va (FVa), cofactors in the activation of factor X and prothrombin, respectively. Protein C levels in the plasma tended to be numerically lower (up to \downarrow 14\%) in concizumab-dosed groups and reached statistical significance on a few occasions prior to Week 52. During the last week of dosing (Week 52), plasma concentrations of Protein C increased significantly (\uparrow 15\%) in MD females (consistent with the lack of an effect on platelet counts) and suggests a decline in pharmacodynamics activity.
(Protein S (PROS) Biomarker): Protein S (a natural anticoagulant) is a vitamin K-dependent plasma glycoprotein synthesized in the liver. Protein S functions as a cofactor to Protein C in the inactivation of Factors Va and VIIIa. Protein S concentrations increased significantly in HD males (\uparrow 33\%) and decreased significantly in LD males and in MD/HD females (\downarrow 14\% to \downarrow 16\%) during Week 6. These results did not correspond sufficiently with
L1414: Parameters	Major Findings
other pharmacodynamic analyses, and the significance of these findings is unclear.
(Antithrombin Biomarker): Antithrombin is the primary inhibitor of thrombin, Factor Xa and Factor IXa. Antithrombin levels in the plasma tended to decline numerically (up to ↓27%) in concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys (intermittently reached statistical significance) and is consistent with the pharmacology of concizumab.
(Thrombin Generation Test - TGT): Plasma samples for TGT were analyzed using a non-validated methodology and the results were stated to be reported separately by the Applicant.

Clinical chemistry	
There were no notable changes in clinical chemistry parameters in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (≤ 9 mg/kg/day) for 52-weeks.

Free TFPI biomarker
Residual TFPI functionality (activity)	
Blood samples for the free TFPI were collected on Weeks -2, -1, Day 1, Week 6, 13, 26, 39 and 52. A dose-dependent reduction in the level of free TFPI (not bound to concizumab) following the administration of concizumab was observed in cynomolgus monkeys. The levels of free TFPI were below the lower limit of detection (12.6 ng/mL) for the majority of MD/HD cynomolgus monkeys from Week 6 forward. Free TFPI levels tended to be higher in males. These findings are consistent with the proposed mechanism of action, whereby concizumab binds specifically to the Kunitz-domain-2 of the TFPI and prevents TFPI from binding to and blocking the active site of the coagulation factor Xa (FXa).
Blood samples for the chromogenic plasma TFPI activity were collected on Weeks -2, -1, Day 1, Week 6, 9, 13, 17, 21, 26, 30, 34, 39, 44 and 48 from cynomolgus monkeys. A high degree of variability was observed in the chromogenic activity assay (U/mL) that attempted to quantitate the residual TFPI functionality/activity in the plasma of cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab. Assay values tended to be lower in concizumab-dosed groups (when adjusted for individual baseline levels) and suggests that the plasma TFPI activity may have been reduced by concizumab. The development of anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) tended to correspond to an increase in chromogenic (TFPI) activity and a decline in systemic exposure indicating that the ADAs produced in cynomolgus monkeys were likely inhibitory in nature.
Total TFPI (free TFPI and TFPI bound to concizumab) was analyzed using a non-validated methodology and the results were stated to be reported separately by the Applicant.
L1415: Parameters	Major Findings
Anti-drug antibody (ADA) analysis	Anti-drug antibodies were detectable in LD monkeys at Week 6 (67%) and the number of ADA-positive monkeys increased by Week 13 (89%) and remained positive thru Week 26. Cynomolgus monkeys (LD) continuing after the 26-week interim sacrifice (N=9) were all ADA positive between Weeks 34 and Week 52, excluding a LD male (#2007) that was negative during Weeks 44 and 52. At the MD, ADAs were detectable at Week 6 (17%) and the number of ADA-positive monkeys increased by Week 13 (28%) and remained positive thru Week 26. Two of the cynomolgus monkeys (MD) continuing after the 26-week interim sacrifice (20%) were ADA positive thru Week 52. Anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) were not detected in HD monkeys and suggests that higher concentrations of concizumab in the plasma may interfere with the ADA assay. It's likely that the actual number of ADA positive monkeys at the MD was higher and that ADAs were present at the HD but not detectable due to deficiencies in ADA detection assay methodology.
Toxicokinetic	The toxicokinetic evaluation indicated that systemic exposure to concizumab was affected by the presence of anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) at the LD/MD.
After the first administration, t_{max} (median) was reached at 6 hrs or 24 hrs (post dose). After repeat-dose administration (Weeks 13, 26 and 52), t_{max} (median) ranged from 0 hrs to 24 hrs. Concizumab exposures (C_{max} and AUC_{0-24hrs}) tended to be lower in females and increased in a greater than dose-proportional manner, particularly at the LD/MD (non-linear pharmacokinetics). Concizumab accumulation was observed at all doses but was particularly notable at the LD/MD where individual accumulation values (Rac_{Obs}) of up to 639 (MD Male - #3006) were observed.
Gross pathology	(Thrombi): Thrombus (dark focus on the left caudal lobe of the lung) was observed grossly in 1 HD female (#4502) at Week 13. No gross pathological findings were noted in cynomolgus monkeys after 26 weeks of dosing (\le 9 mg/kg/day) with concizumab. Thrombus (mass) was observed grossly in the heart of 1 HD female (#4513) and in the lung of 1 MD female (#3505) at Week 52. The formation of thrombi is consistent with the pro-coagulative effects of concizumab in normal healthy cynomolgus monkeys.
Organ weights	Unremarkable.
L1416: Histopathology	(Thrombosis): Histopathological evaluations revealed thrombosis affecting single blood vessels (small to medium sized arteries and occasional veins) in the lung, heart, axillary lymph node and liver of 3 HD cynomolgus monkeys terminated during the 13-Week interim sacrifice. Histopathological evaluations revealed thrombosis (minimal to moderate) was affecting single blood vessels (small to medium sized arteries and occasional veins) in the lung and liver of 1 HD male (#4006) and in the liver/spleen (#4505) or lungs (#4506) of 2 HD females terminated during the 26-Week interim sacrifice. There was no evidence of tissue necrosis in the lung, liver, or spleen in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\le 9 mg/kg/day) for 26-weeks. Histopathological evaluations revealed thrombosis (minimal to marked) was affecting single blood vessels (small to medium sized arteries and occasional veins) in multiple organs systems in 3 MD and 6 HD cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\le 9 mg/kg/day) for 52-weeks. The thrombosis correlated with gross necropsy findings of masses in the heart of 1 HD female (#4513) and in the lung of 1 MD female (#3505). There was no evidence of necrosis in the tissues affected by thrombosis in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab (\le 9 mg/kg/day) for 52-weeks.
Adequate battery: yes	(Subendothelial Intimal Thickening) Subendothelial intimal thickening (minimal or marked) in isolated small to medium-sized arteries in the lung was observed in 1 HD male (#4002) and 2 HD females (#4502 and #4503) after 13 weeks. Note: Similar findings were reported to have been observed in the large intestine (colon) of 1 HD female (#4503) in the interim study report but was eventually removed from the histopathology descriptions, summary tables, and the individual animal data in the final study report after the Applicant's reanalysis of the data. Subendothelial intimal thickening (moderate) in isolated small to medium-sized arteries in the lung and liver was observed in 1 HD male (#4006) after 26 weeks. Subendothelial intimal thickening (mild to moderate) in isolated small to medium-sized arteries in the lung was observed in 1 MD female (#3505) and 1 HD male (#4012) after 52 weeks. The reversibility of these vascular changes was not assessed, and these findings are consistent with the pharmacological effects of an antihemorrhagic when administered to cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state.
(Tissue Necrosis): The combination of multifocal thrombosis and subendothelial intimal thickening in the left caudal lung lobe resulted in complete luminal obstruction of the pulmonary vasculature in 1 HD female (#4502) at Week 13. This condition was associated with partial necrosis of the left caudal lung lobe, which likely resulted from infarction of the affected area and corresponded with the dark focus observed grossly during necropsy. The reversibility of the vascular changes at the HD (Week 13) was not assessed, and these findings are consistent with the pharmacological effects of an antihemorrhagic when administered to cynomolgus monkeys in a normo-coagulative state.
(Brain): Lymphocytic plasmacytic infiltration (minimal) was present in the choroid plexus of 1 HD male (#4001) at Week 13 and is

L1420: ## 13.1.2.2. Genotoxicity Studies
L1421: Concizumab is a monoclonal antibody (mAb) consisting of naturally occurring amino acids, and is not expected to be genotoxic or to interact directly with DNA or other chromosomal material. There were no mechanistic data submitted which suggested that concizumab had mutagenic or proliferative potential, and as a result, genotoxicity studies were not performed with concizumab.
L1422: ## 13.1.2.3. Carcinogenicity Studies
L1423: As concizumab is not functionally active in rodents, standard regulatory carcinogenicity studies in rats and/or mice would not be expected to provide a valuable assessment of carcinogenicity.
L1424: Therefore, the Applicant conducted a carcinogenicity assessment of concizumab using a weight of evidence approach (Study 321420) that considered the mode of action, a review of the relevant literature (that included an evaluation of &gt;670 publications) and an evaluation of the nonclinical and clinical data, consistent with the guidance provided in ICH S6 (R1). A summary of the Applicant's assessment is provided below.
L1425: - Tumor growth and metastasis is a complex and multi-factorial process and the TF/Factor VII (FVIIa) signaling pathway is known to contribute to this process. However, physiological levels of Tissue Factor Pathway Inhibitor (TFPI) do not appear to influence these reactions. Instead, at physiological levels, TFPI (and by extension concizumab, a TFPI antagonist) influence signaling via TF/FVIIa/FXa and this signaling pathway has not been associated with tumor development.
- The C-terminal part of TFPI has been suggested to play a role in reducing tumor cell proliferation, angiogenesis, and metastasis. These processes are not expected to be affected by the specific binding of concizumab to the K2 domain of TFPI.
- The inhibition of TFPI by concizumab leads to increased thrombin generation downstream in the coagulation process. Thrombin has been reported to mediate signaling reactions implicated in cancer, and these reactions may proceed if/when hemostasis is restored in hemophilia. Therefore, concizumab (and, in general, any procoagulant agent) has the potential to enhance the effects of thrombin, including the prothrombotic state, observed in many types of cancer.
- Tissue Factor Pathway Inhibitor (TFPI) is present in some cancer cell lines at varying expression levels, and increased expression of TFPI would be expected to dampen the procoagulant effects of TF. Therefore, it is conceivable that concizumab (as well as any other procoagulant agent) could enhance the hypercoagulable state often observed with cancer. Treatment of hemophilia patients with concizumab therefore has the potential to increase the risk of thromboembolic events in subjects with undiagnosed malignancies.
- In individuals with partial TFPI deficiency (e.g., due to specific TFPI polymorphisms, factor V (FV) deficiency or intake of pharmaceutical estrogen products, i.e., oral contraceptives), the effect of reduced TFPI on general cancer risk in humans is considered predominantly neutral.
- Concizumab is a first-in-class mAb directed against TFPI. The available literature reports from three other TFPI antibodies in clinical development did not reveal any information related to human cancer risk.
- Concizumab has been evaluated in nonclinical repeat-dose toxicity studies in cynomolgus monkeys with daily subcutaneous administration for up to 52 weeks. In these studies, no signs of proliferation, neovascularization, immunosuppression, or organ weight changes were observed.
L1426: While the reviewer agreed with the Applicant that there were no signs of neovascularization or immunosuppression in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for up to 52 weeks, vascular intimal proliferation/hypertrophy/hyperplasia in the lungs, vascular hypertrophy and/or hyperplasia in the brain (choroid plexus) and/or the presence of a granuloma in the caudate nucleus of the brain were observed in cynomolgus monkeys dosed with concizumab for \ge 13 weeks. While the Applicant suggests that the proliferative changes in the vasculature are likely the consequence of an immunogenic response to (and not a pharmacological effect of) concizumab, the reviewer contends that these changes are relevant to the overall carcinogenicity risk assessment of concizumab.
L1427: - Concizumab is currently being assessed in two clinical Phase 3 studies. The clinical data from the completed Phase 1B study and the two Phase 2 studies as well as serious adverse events reported from the two ongoing Phase 3 studies, have not identified any safety concerns in relation to cancer.
L1428: The Applicant considers the carcinogenic potential of concizumab to be adequately evaluated by this weight of evidence evaluation. It is concluded that treatment with concizumab potentially may enhance the prothrombotic conditions observed in some cancer patients, as would be expected for any other procoagulant agent. However, the assessment has not identified evidence that treatment with concizumab may lead to an increased risk of inducing or enhancing tumor development in humans.
L1429: - The Division determined that no additional nonclinical studies assessing the carcinogenic potential of concizumab were warranted for the indications of hemophilia A and hemophilia B, and the CDER Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee (ECAC) concurred with the Division's recommendation.
L1430: ## 13.1.2.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicity
L1431: No reproductive and developmental toxicity studies were conducted with concizumab as the intended population is male hemophilia patients, and the clinical development program was conducted exclusively in males. Hemophilia A is inherited in an X-linked recessive manner and the disease which occurs predominantly in males (99%) is extremely rare in females. Concizumab is also being indicated for Hemophilia B (Factor IX deficiency or Christmas disease, caused by mutations in the coagulation factor IX (FIX) gene on the X chromosome) and hence, is predominantly observed in males (some females who carry the gene may display mild or, rarely, severe symptoms of bleeding).
L1432: ## 13.1.2.4.1. Fertility and Early Embryonic Development
L1433: Consistent with the guidance provided in ICH S6 (R1), specialized evaluations of fertility parameters were included in the 26-week chronic toxicity study conducted in sexually mature cynomolgus monkeys (Study 211087 in Section 13.1.2.1).
L1434: There were no notable effects on the menstrual cycle duration, the length, width and volume of the testis, total sperm count, sperm motility or sperm morphology in cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab at \le 9 mg/kg/day (SC) or 30 mg/kg/week (IV) for 26 weeks.
L1435: Histopathological evaluations of the reproductive organs conducted during the repeat-dose toxicity studies (\le 52 weeks, GLP) in juvenile and sexually mature male and female cynomolgus monkeys did not indicate any adverse effects on the reproductive organs.
L1436: ## 13.1.2.4.2. Embryo Fetal Development
L1437: In mice, inactivation of the TFPI-gene has been shown to result in embryonic lethality (Huang et al. 1997; Ellery et al. 2015) and total TFPI deficiency has not been described in humans (Wood et al. 2014). The Applicant suggests that, as placental transfer of IgG is limited during organogenesis in humans, these findings may not be relevant when administering a monoclonal IgG antibody (i.e., concizumab) to humans (DeSesso et al. 2012).
L1438: It should be noted that an association of recurrent pregnancy loss was observed in women who exhibited TFPI deficiency in combination with an Activated Protein C (APC) resistance phenotype, and in women with anti-TFPI antibodies in combination with antiphospholipid antibodies, suggesting that TFPI may be critical to the maintenance of normal pregnancy in humans (Martinuzzo et al. 2005; Gardiner et al. 2006).
L1439: The reviewer agrees with the Applicant's assessment that the implications of blocking TFPI functionality with a monoclonal antibody in pregnant women are not clear.
L1440: ## 13.1.2.4.3. Pre- and Postnatal Development
L1441: No pre- and postnatal development studies have been conducted with concizumab as the hemophilia patient population is almost exclusively male.
L1442: ## 13.1.2.4.4. Juvenile Animal Studies
L1443: Dedicated juvenile animal studies were not conducted with concizumab, consistent with the guidance provided in ICH M3 (R2)2.
L1444: The Applicant contends that the hemostatic system was the only target organ identified during toxicity assessments. However, the reviewer does not agree with this statement based on the extensive immune-related and vascular toxicity observed in cynomolgus monkeys. The human hemostatic system matures during the early weeks and months of life, and the concentrations of most hemostatic proteins, both in full-term and preterm infants, are comparable to adult values by 6 months of age (Albisetti 2003; Chalmers 2004).
L1445: Consequently, the Applicant argues that the toxicity of concizumab has been adequately characterized through the general toxicology program in juvenile (2 to 3 years of age) and sexually mature (3.5 to 6 years of age) cynomolgus monkeys.
L1446: Studies in neonatal and infant monkeys are technically challenging and are unlikely to identify potential reactions that were not identified from studies in fully developed (adult) animals.
L1447: 2 https://www.fda.gov/regulatory-information/search-fda-guidance-documents/m3r2-nonclinical-safety-studies-conduct-human-clinical-trials-and-marketing-authorization
L1448: Therefore, the reviewer agrees that dedicated juvenile toxicity studies in animals are not considered warranted for the indications of hemophilia A and hemophilia B.

L1477: ## 13.1.2.5.3. Mechanistic Analyses
L1478: ## Separation of Concizumab High Molecular Weight Proteins (HMWPs) by Size Exclusion Chromatography (Study 215027, non-GLP)
L1479: ## Key Study Findings
L1480: - Concizumab (batch ATfK505001) was separated into monomer, dimer, trimer and multimer fractions to allow for detailed biological characterization of concizumab derived HMWP.
L1481: *Reviewer Comments: The material assessed in the 26-week cynomolgus monkey toxicity study contained increased levels of high molecular weight proteins (HMWPs) that consisted predominantly of concizumab multimers. In contrast, the material used in the 4-, 13- and 52-week toxicity studies was comparable to the clinical material and contained significantly lower amounts of HMWPs. This study is part of the evaluations that were conducted to assess whether the change in impurity profile could account for the increased toxicity observed in the 26-week cynomolgus monkey study.*
L1482: **Table 88. Study Information, Study 215027, Non-GLP**
L1483: Study Features and Methods	Details
GLP compliance	No
Objective	The preclinical batch of concizumab (ATfK505001) contained 12.8% high molecular weight protein (HMWP) comprising a heterogeneous mixture of species with molecular weights ranging from 300 kDa (corresponding to a dimer) to at least 1 MDa. In order to enable a more detailed biological characterization of concizumab HMWPs, sub-populations of HMWPs were isolated with more narrow size distributions.
Methods	Concizumab (Batch ATfK505001) was subjected to size exclusion chromatography (SEC) and separated into monomer, dimer, trimer, and higher order multimer fractions. The size distribution of the individual fractions was assessed by size exclusion high performance liquid chromatography multi-angle light scattering (SE-HPLC-MALS) analysis. Dimer, trimer and multimer species were found to be relatively stable, i.e., only minor dissociation into monomer was observed upon handling and storage.
L1484: Source: Reviewer constructed summary table of the toxicology Study (215027) submitted to the BLA.
L1485: Abbreviations: GLP, good laboratory practices; HMWP, high molecular weight protein; SEC, size exclusion chromatography; SE-HPLC-MALS, size exclusion high performance liquid chromatography multi-angle light scattering
L1486: ## **Separation Analysis of Anti-Concizumab Antibodies by Size Exclusion Chromatography and Immune Complex (IC) Enzyme-linked Immunoassay (ELISA) in Cynomolgus Monkey Plasma Samples Derived from the 26-Week and 52-Week Toxicity Studies (Study 214449, non-GLP)**
L1487: ## **Key Study Findings**
L1488: - Immune complexes (ADA/concizumab) of different sizes developed over time (compared to pre-dose values) in all concizumab dosed (26 and 52 week treatment duration) cynomolgus monkeys. In the control group, no immune complex formation was observed over time.
- There was a trend toward more immune complexes of a high molecular weight in samples derived from cynomolgus monkeys in the 26-week toxicity study (versus the 52-week study) that employed concizumab with an increased level of high molecular weight proteins (HMWPs).
- In the 52-week toxicity study there was a trend towards larger immune complexes at the LD (compared to controls, MD, and HD).
L1489: *Reviewer Comments: The assay for qualitative detection of ADA/concizumab ICs was considered acceptable and batches of concizumab enriched in multimers tended to form ICs more efficiently in cynomolgus monkeys.*
L1490: **Table 89. Study Information, Study 214449, Non-GLP**
L1491: Study Features and Methods	Details
GLP compliance	No
Objective	The purpose of the study was to evaluate the presence of anti-concizumab antibodies (anti-drug antibodies, ADAs) in complex with concizumab (without interference from free concizumab) in plasma samples derived from the 26-week (211087) and 52-week (213542) toxicity studies in cynomolgus monkeys.
Methods	The present study evaluated samples from cynomolgus monkeys with vascular lesions detected by histopathology as well as samples from controls. Detection of ADA/concizumab immune complexes without interference from free concizumab was achieved by separation of the complexes from the free drug by size exclusion chromatography (SEC) followed by an ELISA that was specific for complexes of ADA/concizumab.
L1492: Source: Reviewer constructed summary table of the toxicology Study (214449) submitted to the BLA.

L1494: ## **Immunohistochemistry Study to Investigate Biodistribution of Concizumab-Related Immune Complexes in Tissues Derived from Cynomolgus Monkeys Administered Concizumab for 26 Weeks (Study 214391, non-GLP)**
L1495: ## **Key Study Findings**
L1496: - Granular deposits containing concizumab, monkey IgG, IgM, and/or complement component C3 (C3) were observed in a subset of the affected choroid plexus blood vessels or associated macrophages, respectively, in 2/4 (50%) and 3/4 (75%) concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys with choroid plexus endothelial cell hypertrophy, hyperplasia and/or vasculitis.
- Similar granular deposits were observed in affected lung blood vessels of 3/5 (60%) concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys with lung vascular/perivascular alterations.
L1497: *Reviewer Comments: The presence of granular deposits at vascular and/or inflammatory sites in the lung and choroid plexus of cynomolgus monkeys is consistent with a role of immune complexes in the pathogenesis of these lesions. As only a subset of the affected vessels contained granular deposits, it cannot be ruled out that other factors may have contributed to the histologic alterations in the choroid plexus/lung.*
L1498: **Table 90. Study Information, Study 214391, Non-GLP**
L1499: Study Features and Methods	Details
GLP compliance	No
Objective	This report presents the immunohistochemistry (IHC) findings in cynomolgus monkeys dosed for 26 weeks either daily at 0, 3 or 9 mg/kg by subcutaneous administration or at 30 mg/kg by once weekly by intravenous administration (Study 211087). The purpose of the IHC phase of the study was to provide information regarding the biodistribution of concizumab and the formation/deposition of immune complexes
L1500: Study Features and Methods	Details
Methods	
(ICs) containing concizumab, monkey IgG, IgM, and/or C3 in vascular tissues in the brain (including choroid plexus), lung, liver, and/or kidney from selected control and concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys.
Hematoxylin and eosin (H&E) slides were prepared and evaluated for histopathological alterations including subendothelial intimal thickening, vascular/perivascular inflammation, endothelial cell hypertrophy and/or hyperplasia, and thrombosis.
Based on the H&E evaluation, the brain and lung tissues with confirmed vascular/perivascular alterations and corresponding tissues from controls were selected for IHC and were stained for the presence of human IgG (indicative of concizumab), monkey immunoglobulins (IgG and IgM), and/or complement component C3. Granular deposits of human IgG, monkey IgG and IgM, and/or C3 were considered evidence of immune complex deposition.
L1501: Study Features and Methods	Details
Results	
(Choroid Plexus)
Granular deposits were observed (IHC) in intima/media of some affected blood vessels and/or intramural and/or perivascular macrophage infiltrates, respectively, in 2/4 and 3/4 dosed cynomolgus monkeys with endothelial cell hypertrophy and/or hyperplasia or vasculitis in choroid plexus. The granular deposits were either associated with the surface of the macrophages or had been phagocytosed by the macrophages. Granular deposits were not observed in controls or in two concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys without vascular alterations in choroid plexus.
In affected blood vessels, the granular deposits were observed in or near the affected portions of the intima/media (typical sites for immune complex deposition), whereas granular deposits were not observed in unaffected blood vessels. Probable granular deposit localization at vascular branch points was identified in a LD (3 mg/kg/day) male (#8) and were characteristic of immune complex deposition.
The vascular wall granular deposits were composed of co-localized concizumab (human IgG), monkey IgG and/or IgM (likely ADA) but did not contain C3. In choroid plexus macrophages, the granular deposits contained human IgG and IgM and/or C3. It's unclear whether the differences in granular deposit composition between the vascular wall and the macrophages reflected differential processing or a differential uptake by macrophages.
(Lung)
Granular deposits containing concizumab, monkey IgG, IgM, and/or C3 were observed in the intima/media of a subset of affected blood vessels in 3/5 concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys with vascular/perivascular inflammation or other alterations in lung blood vessels but were not observed in controls without vascular and/or perivascular inflammation.
The granular deposits were observed in intima/internal elastic lamina and/or at vascular branch points of relatively early lesions in lung in a LD male (#6) and in a 30 mg/kg IV dosed male (#17). Concizumab-, monkey IgG-, and IgM-containing granular deposits were also observed in at least one thrombus in the 30 mg/kg IV dosed male (#17). In a 30 mg/kg (IV) dosed male (#18), the granular deposits associated with the intimal thickening were composed of IgM and C3, however the presence of concizumab was not detected.
L1502: Source: Reviewer constructed summary table of the toxicology Study (214391) submitted to the BLA.
Abbreviations: ADA, anti-drug antibody; C3, component 3; GLP, good laboratory practices; H&amp;E, hematoxylin and eosin; IgG, immunoglobulin G; IgM, immunoglobulin M; IHC, immunohistochemistry; LD, low dose.
L1503: ## **Analysis of Complement Activation and Circulating Immune Complexes in Samples Derived from the 52-Week Cynomolgus Monkey Toxicity Study (Study 214471, non-GLP)**
L1504: ## **Key Study Findings**
L1505: - Circulating immune complexes (CIC) were present in all concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys at Week 26 (prior to dose administration) and were significantly elevated at the HD. Following the administration of concizumab (Week 26), significant increases in CIC levels were observed at 4 hours (post-dose) in all concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys.
- Classical complement pathway activity (50% Hemolytic Complement equivalent, CH50) was not notably affected in blood sample derived from cynomolgus monkeys administered concizumab for 26-/52-weeks (\le 9 mg/kg/day).
- The results of the complement fragment C3a assay were inconclusive at Week 26, and there were no notable effects observed at Week 52.
L1506: *Reviewer Comments: The assays for CIC, CH50 and C3a were considered acceptable and indicated that concizumab elicited the formation of CICs but did not increase complement activation in cynomolgus monkeys.*
L1507: **Table 91. Study Information, Study 214471, Non-GLP**
L1508: Study Features and Methods	Details
GLP compliance	No
Objective	The purpose of the study was to investigate complement activation in samples from the 52-week cynomolgus monkey toxicity Study (213542) using three different assays.
Methods	Samples were obtained during Week 26 and Week 52 when steady state plasma concentrations of concizumab had been established. Circulating immune complexes (CIC) were assessed using an enzyme immunoassay (EIA) that measures circulating immune complexes containing C3 fragments. Classical complement pathway activity (50% Hemolytic Complement equivalent (CH50)) was assessed using an EIA that measures the total classical complement pathway activity. Complement fragment C3a was assessed in an EIA where C3a binds to microwell plates coated with a monoclonal antibody against C3a. Analyses were performed on samples collected at pre-dose, 4-, 8- and 24-hours after dosing (SC).

L1511: ## **Evaluation of Endothelial Activation Markers Induced by Different Batches of Concizumab (Study CNGU130901, Non-GLP)**
L1512: ## **Key Study Findings**
L1513: - Concizumab (monomeric) or multimers of concizumab did not influence the cell surface expression of ICAM-1, E-selectin, or VCAM-1 in HUVEC cells and neither form of
L1514: concizumab acted in synergy with the cytokines (TNF-\alpha/IL-1\beta) to induce a further up-regulation.

Note: These Fc\gamma-receptor were not expressed on resting HUVEC cells, and these cytokines (TNF-\alpha/IL-1\beta) alone were unable to trigger their expression (in vitro).
L1516: *Reviewer Comments: The levels of endothelial cell activation were comparable between monomeric and multimeric batches of concizumab and suggest that aggregates of concizumab do not elicit biological effects that are different from the parent (monomeric) protein (in vitro).*

L1521: ## **Immunohistochemistry Study to Investigate Biodistribution of Concizumab-Related Immune Complexes in Tissues from Cynomolgus Monkeys Administered Concizumab for 13, 26, or 52 weeks (Study 214400, non-GLP)**

L1526: **Table 93. Study Information, Study 214400, Non-GLP**
L1527: Study Features and Methods	Details
GLP compliance	No
Objective	The purpose of the IHC phase of the study was to provide information regarding concizumab biodistribution and the formation/deposition of immune complexes (ICs) containing concizumab, monkey IgG, IgM, and/or complement component C3. This report presents the IHC findings in cynomolgus monkeys from Study 213542 and contains the data from selected cynomolgus monkeys dosed for 13-, 26-, and 52-weeks.
Methods	Hematoxylin and eosin (H&E) slides were prepared and evaluated for histopathological alterations. Based on the H&E evaluation, the lung, liver, and brain-choroid plexus with vascular/inflammatory changes (subendothelial intimal thickening/plasmacytic/histiocytic infiltration) were selected for IHC and were stained for the presence of human IgG (indicative of concizumab), monkey immunoglobulins (IgG and IgM), and/or complement component C3. Granular deposits of human IgG, monkey IgG and IgM, and/or C3 were considered evidence of immune complex deposition.
L1528: ## Results
L1529: ## (13-Week Interim Sacrifice)
L1530: Granular deposits containing concizumab, IgM, and/or C3 were observed in a subset of affected blood vessels but not in any unaffected blood vessels in the lungs of 1/3 (33%) of the dosed cynomolgus monkeys with lung vascular and/or perivascular alterations. All three (100%) cynomolgus monkeys had resolving or chronic lesions (days to weeks old) characterized by intimal thickening while a single HD female (4502) had active (days old) lung lesions characterized by coagulative necrosis, thrombosis, and neutrophilic/mononuclear cell infiltration. In this female, granular deposits were observed in the intima/media of the vascular wall and/or at branch points of a subset of actively affected blood vessels with ongoing inflammation (consistent with immune complex-mediated vascular alterations). In this same female, granular deposits were also observed in neutrophils, monocytes/macrophages, and/or alveolar macrophages associated with the vascular lesions and likely represent phagocytosis and/or processing of immune complexes.
L1531: Granular deposits composed of concizumab, IgG, and C3 were also observed in the choroid plexus stroma in 1/2 (50%) of the concizumab-dosed cynomolgus monkeys that presented with inflammatory cell infiltration in the choroid plexus. No granular deposits were observed in the 13-week interim controls.
L1532: ## (26-Week Interim Sacrifice)
L1533: Granular deposits were observed in the choroid plexus of 2/4 (50%) of the cynomolgus monkeys that presented with inflammatory alterations in the choroid plexus. The alterations consisted of inflammatory cell infiltration (including plasma cells) in 1 MD male (3002) where the granular deposits were associated with the intima/media of blood vessels. In a HD female (4506) lymphocytic and/or plasmacytic infiltration and granular deposits were located in the choroid plexus stroma.
L1534: Various stages of vascular/perivascular alterations including thrombosis (occasionally with partial resolution) and pleural inflammation were observed in the lungs and/or liver in a single HD male (4006). No clear granular deposits were observed in this male; however, macrophage phagocytosis of concizumab, monkey IgG, IgM, and C3-coated erythrocytes and platelets were present in the lungs. This male also presented with increases in intraluminal staining of concizumab, monkey IgG, IgM, and C3 in resolving and/or recanalizing thrombi in the liver and lungs. These observations may indicate macrophage processing of immune complexes in the lung and possible local intravascular apposition and/or formation of immune complexes in liver and lung. No granular deposits were observed in the 26-week interim controls.
L1535: Study Features and Methods	Details
(52-Week Terminal Sacrifice)
Granular deposits were observed in the choroid plexus of 4/7 (57%) of the cynomolgus monkeys that presented with inflammatory alterations in the choroid plexus after 52-weeks. All four of these cynomolgus monkeys [1 LD male (2007) 1 LD female (2507), 1 MD female (3505) and 1 HD female (4510)] presented with lymphoplasmacytic infiltrates that were associated with monkey IgG, IgM, and/or C3 containing granular deposits in the choroid plexus stroma. Two of these cynomolgus monkeys [1 LD female (2507) and 1 MD female (3505)] also presented with associated eosinophilic infiltrates.
Granular deposits containing concizumab (human IgG), IgM, and/or C3 were observed in a subset of affected blood vessels but not in any unaffected blood vessels in 2/2 (100%) of the cynomolgus monkeys that presented with lung vascular and/or perivascular alterations. The alterations in the pulmonary vessels of these two cynomolgus monkeys [1 MD female (3505) and 1 HD female (4012)] were comparable to those observed in HD males and females after 13-weeks and 26-weeks. No granular deposits were observed in the 52-week controls.
L1536: Source: Reviewer constructed summary table of the toxicology Study (214400) submitted to the BLA
L1537: Abbreviations: C3, component 3; GLP, good laboratory practices; HD, high dose; H&amp;E, hematoxylin and eosin; IC, immune complexes; IgG, immunoglobulin G; IgM, immunoglobulin M; IHC, immunohistochemistry; LD, low dose; MD, mid dose.
L1538: ## **In Vitro Functional Characterization of Multimer and Monomer Forms of Concizumab (Study CCOC130901, non-GLP)**
L1539: ## Key Study Findings
L1540: - A minimal (albeit significant) increase in the half maximal inhibitory concentration (IC_{50}) was observed for the multimeric form of concizumab in a FXa generation assay (in the presence or absence of 25\muM phospholipids) and suggests that the TFPI neutralizing capacity of the high molecular weight protein (HMWP) form of concizumab may be reduced relative to the monomeric form.
- In contrast, when FXa generation was assessed using endothelial cells that expressed surface associated TF and TFPI, a lower half maximal inhibitory concentration (IC_{50})(non-significant) was achieved with the multimeric form of concizumab relative to the monomeric form.
- In a thrombin generation assay (employing platelet-poor plasma with added phospholipids) significantly higher procoagulant activity (thrombin peak height) was achieved with the multimeric form of concizumab (P1) relative to the monomeric form (P4) and this effect was enhanced (dose dependently) as the level of TF was reduced. Thrombin peak heights were
L1541: comparable between the multimeric and monomeric forms of concizumab when employing platelet-rich plasma in the thrombin generation assay.
L1542: - The procoagulant effects in whole blood were comparable between the multimeric form and the monomeric form of concizumab when assessed by thromboelastography.
L1543: *Reviewer Comments: The study suggests that the multimeric form of concizumab can elicit altered biological effects (relative to the monomeric form) in blood coagulation assays under certain in vitro conditions (e.g., a thrombin generation assay employing platelet-poor plasma and low TF concentrations as a catalyst).*
L1544: **Table 94. Study Information, Study CCOC130901, Non-GLP**
L1545: Study Features and Methods	Details
GLP compliance	No
Objective	The purpose of the study was to evaluate the effect of concizumab containing different amounts of high molecular weight proteins (HMWPs) in blood coagulation assays.
Methods	The antibodies assessed included concizumab (2% HMWP), concizumab multimer (92.9 % HMWP), size exclusion chromatography isolated concizumab monomer P4 (0.5 % HMWP) and size exclusion chromatography isolated concizumab multimer P1 (90% HMWP). The ability of concizumab to neutralize FXa inhibition through TFPI was assessed in a FXa generation assay, the activity of concizumab in plasma was assessed using a thrombin generation assay and the activity in whole blood was evaluated using thromboelastography.

L1548: ## **Dose Formulation Analysis of Return Samples from Toxicity Studies (211087 and 213542) Conducted with Concizumab in Cynomolgus Monkeys (Study 215023, non-GLP)**
L1549: ## Key Study Findings
L1550: - Minimal (negligible) changes in the content of monomeric and multimeric forms of concizumab were observed in the dose formulation samples over the 26- and 52-week observation periods, when stored at or below -70°C. A similar result was observed when dose formulation samples were stored at 5°C for 7 days prior to storage at or below -70°C.
